  - `mkdir` sits above the `try:` and raised straight out of `SyncManager.__init__` (a raw traceback on the sync page); it is now flagged the same way.
- **`UnicodeDecodeError` is a SIBLING of `json.JSONDecodeError`, not a subclass** (both are `ValueError`). `except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError)` therefore let it escape and **crash the sync page outright** - one editor re-saving a config file in a Danish ANSI codepage is enough, and these files are full of `æøå`.
- **"Unreadable" is not "empty", and `load → mutate → save` turns the second into permanent loss.** `core/library.py:_update` and `shared/helpers.py:atomic_update_sync_pairs` both degraded a failed read to `[]`/default and then WROTE, replacing every saved pair, group, name and daily-sync membership with whatever that one call happened to add. Now split by cause: **damaged content** is quarantined to `*.corrupt.json` first (the `core.preset_manager` pattern, so the data survives on disk); a **transient `OSError`** aborts the write entirely (`_READ_FAILED`), because there is nothing wrong with the file. `library._save` also reports failure instead of swallowing it - a full disk made every rename appear to work and silently revert.

## `\\?\` disables path parsing, so what you hand it must already be canonical
`shared/helpers.make_long_path` was emitting two shapes the kernel rejects (both measured 2026-08-06, both reachable from the UI):
- **UNC**: `\\server\share\f` became `\\?\\\server\share\f` → **WinError 123** ("syntax is incorrect") on *every* open, stat and sqlite connect. The correct form is `\\?\UNC\server\share\f`. Diagnostic worth reusing: assert on the error **KIND** against a non-existent host - malformed gives 123, well-formed gives the ordinary WinError 53. **The probe hostname must contain no underscore** - an underscore is illegal in a hostname, so Windows returns the very 123 the test reads as failure, and the test fails against *correct* code.
- **Forward slashes**: `\\?\C:/Users/x` does not resolve at all - `exists()` returns **False** and `open` says "No such file or directory", so the file looks *absent* rather than erroring. Tk's `askdirectory` (the fallback folder picker, used whenever the COM shell picker fails) returns forward slashes on Windows.
- `normpath` fixes both and also resolves `.` / `..`, which the prefix would otherwise take literally. The `Path(s).is_absolute()` gate is kept deliberately: it is False for a drive-relative root like `\Users\x`, which cannot be an extended-length path at all - widening it to `ntpath.isabs()` would emit the invalid `\\?\\Users\x`.
- **`core/sync_manager.py` had its OWN COPY of this function**, and all 26 manifest call sites used that copy - so the fix reached none of the sync database until the duplicate was made an alias. Two tests now pin it: one asserts the two agree on every probe, one bans a hand-rolled `'\\?\' + path` anywhere (`converters/archive.py` had one). **A shared path primitive with two implementations is a fix that lands on half the app, silently.**

## Nothing may park the app on a number a server chose, or on a process that stopped talking
- **`Retry-After` is attacker/misconfiguration-controlled** and was obeyed literally: a header of `86400` parked the download on a one-second cancel-polling loop for a **day**. `core.canvas_logic.parse_retry_after` clamps to `MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS` (120) and is used by BOTH engines (`sync/execution.py` had its own hand-rolled `int()`). It returns an **int** because the value is both shown to the user ("retry in 3s") and counted out a second at a time - and because the producer formats it into `RATE_LIMIT:{wait}` which the consumer parses back, where a float made `int()` raise *inside the handler meant to absorb the error*.
- **ffmpeg gets no timeout flags** (a version that rejects them would break every Panopto download), and the poll loop had no wall-clock bound - so a stream that simply stopped sending hung for ever. Interactively the user can cancel; **the daily auto-sync runs unattended**. `panopto/stream.py` now bounds it with `_STALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` measured as **growth of the `.part` file**, not elapsed time, so a genuinely slow download is never killed. `_stop_ffmpeg` terminates *then reaps* - `kill()` without a following `wait()` leaves the child unreaped and may hold the handle that makes the `.part` cleanup a silent no-op.
- **A test for "this terminates" must be able to FAIL rather than hang.** The first version called the download directly, so removing the watchdog made the test hang for the full 900s mutation timeout instead of reporting. It now runs on a thread with a join timeout.

## Post-processing: a phase is a sibling, and BOOKKEEPING must not undo finished work
- **One phase failing must not cancel the ones after it.** The nine `run_*` conversion runners are independent - a wedged COM server has no bearing on HTML→Markdown - but only `run_excel_data_conversion` had a per-item handler and `run_all_conversions` called all nine bare. One unexpected exception therefore took out every later phase **and `retry_failed_conversions`**, which is the thing that would otherwise have recovered the files. `_run_phase(runner, items, ui)` is the single guard, at the boundary the phases are already independent across; it cannot be forgotten when a tenth converter is added, and a test asserts every call site goes through it. It also clears `active_file_placeholder`, which the runners only do on their normal exit - otherwise the line stays stuck on whichever file blew up.
- **`_update_manifest_path` must never raise.** By the time it runs the converted file is on disk and the original is deleted; all that is left is repointing a manifest row. But `load_manifest` deliberately RE-RAISES database errors ("Aborting to prevent data loss"), so a manifest briefly locked - by antivirus, or by the sync that has just finished writing to it - aborted the whole conversion phase mid-way. The trade is explicit: swallowing it costs one stale row that the next sync's heal pass fixes, propagating it costs every remaining file in the phase. Logged at warning, never silent.
- Note the interaction with the sqlite classification above: making a transient DB error *stop resetting the manifest* means `_db_init_failed` is now reachable where it previously was not, and `load_manifest` raises on it. That is the intended trade (loud beats silent), but it is only safe because post-processing no longer treats a manifest failure as fatal.

### …and the repoint must not depend on how the path is SPELLED (2026-08-21)
Measured by the live macOS audit driving the PACKAGED app through a real run of course 43660: **62 of the 63 Office files converted in that run left their manifest row pointing at the source the converter had just deleted.** The one that repointed was an Excel file - and `converters/excel.py` is the only one of the three Office converters that does not call `resolve()` on the path it returns.
- **`Path.relative_to` is a STRING operation.** `converters/pdf.py` and `converters/word.py` both return `str(dst.resolve().absolute())` from their macOS branch while `sm.local_path` carries whatever spelling the destination was CONFIGURED with. Wherever the course folder is reached through a symlink - `/tmp` → `/private/tmp`, or a course folder linked onto an external drive, which is an ordinary thing for the small-SSD student this product is aimed at - the two spellings differ, `relative_to` raises, and **both** call sites swallowed it without a word. Same app, same course, same converter, silently different outcome depending on how the destination happened to be spelled.
- **It is not untidy bookkeeping, and the sync engine's own code says why.** `analyze_course`'s missing-local-file branch carries an explicit **URL Compiler bypass** (`.url`/`.webloc` gone while `convert_urls` is on → `uptodate_files`) and an **Archive Extraction bypass** - because those two converters are many→one and one→many and *cannot* repoint. There is deliberately **no such bypass for Office**, because Office is 1:1 and is supposed to repoint. So a stale Office row falls to `locally_deleted_files` and is re-offered as a **restore** on every later sync, its PDF sits untracked, and a re-download plus re-conversion then overwrites that PDF - which is how a student's annotated copy is lost.
- **`_course_relative(sm, path)` is the one primitive** both sites use: try the paths as given, then compare **realpaths** - the one spelling both sides can always agree on. `os.path.realpath` rather than `Path.resolve` because it is defined on a path that no longer EXISTS, which `original_file` never does by then (every source-consuming converter deletes its source before reporting success).
- **Both silent paths are now loud, because the silence is why it shipped**: a path genuinely outside the course warns, and a lookup that matched no row is logged at debug instead of being indistinguishable from a successful repoint. A conversion whose source was never tracked (an extracted archive member, a secondary-content render) is ORDINARY, so that one is debug and not a warning.
- **The mutation pass found the fix INCOMPLETE, and that is the reusable part.** `_resolve_conversion_target`'s "is the recorded product beside the source" test compared **absolute** parents (`prod_path.parent == src.parent`), so a resolved `src` still disabled the ownership check even once the relativisation was fixed - and that check is the one that diverts to `_NewVersion` when the student has annotated the product. Both comparisons are now course-relative (`PurePosixPath(prod_rel).parent == PurePosixPath(src_rel).parent`).
- **The user-visible consequence is MEASURED, not reasoned.** A live sync analysis of the packaged run's folder renders **"63 Deleted locally"** on the review screen - 0/63 selected, Smart Select offering them as DOC / PPTM / PPTX - which are exactly the 63 Office sources the conversion consumed. Header: *"63 files pending sync, 199 files up to date"*. Nothing re-downloads silently (the boxes default unchecked), so it is a reporting defect rather than data loss - but it recurs on every sync, and "Select All here" is the natural response to being told 63 files are missing, which re-downloads ~180 MB of PowerPoints and then overwrites the PDFs the user already has.
- Covered by `tests/test_conversion_repoint_spelling.py` (12); all **8** mutations caught (`scripts/_mutate_conversion_repoint.py`), so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite. **Two of those mutants were surviving gaps in my own tests** - one asserted a rootless manager only for a path outside the cwd, and there was no test at all for the ownership site.

## `office_safe_path` is FOR long paths - so its own I/O has to be long-path safe
The context manager exists only for sources ≥ 240 chars (Office COM hard-crashes on them), and then read the source and wrote the destination **without the prefix** - so on a default Windows install, where `LongPathsEnabled` is 0, the one function meant to handle long paths could not read or write them. Fixed via `make_long_path` on the shadow copy, the destination `mkdir` and the move-back.
- **The asymmetry is the whole point and a test pins it**: OUR file operations need the prefix; the paths we **yield** must not have it, because Office COM chokes on a `\\?\` path too. That is why the shadowing exists at all.
- **Not reproducible on a machine with `LongPathsEnabled=1`** (this dev box has it), which is exactly why it survived: everything passes locally that fails on a stock install. Same reasoning and same fix as the note in `panopto/stream.py`. When a path-length fix "can't be reproduced", check that registry key before concluding there is no bug.

## Deleting the SOURCE is the one irreversible step - audit the six as a FAMILY
Six converters delete the file they converted FROM. The pattern was correct in three (`code.py`, `md.py`, `url.py` - fsync, then verify, then delete) and absent in three, which is precisely how it stayed unnoticed: any single file read as fine.
- **`video.py` was the worst of them.** `conversion_success = True` was set the instant `write_audiofile` RETURNED, with nothing looking at the result, and the `finally` then deleted the source. `write_audiofile` drives ffmpeg through a pipe and can return having produced no usable audio. The file deleted on that evidence is a lecture VIDEO - the largest and least replaceable artifact the app handles, and for a Panopto capture possibly not re-downloadable at all. **The check has to happen before the `return`, not in the `finally`**: by the time the finally runs the function has already returned the mp3 path, so the caller records a success and repoints the manifest at a file that is not there. Returning `None` leaves `conversion_success` False, so the video is kept and the failure is honest.
- **`archive.py` deleted the zip without checking anything came out.** Both paths can produce nothing while raising nothing: `_filter_zip_members` strips every `__MACOSX/` and `._*` entry, and tarfile's `data` filter SILENTLY SKIPS members it deems unsafe. An archive whose whole content is filtered away therefore left an empty folder and no archive.
- `converters/verify.py` holds both gates - `pdf_looks_real` (magic + size) and the generic `file_has_content(path, min_bytes)`. Err LOW on the floor: a false negative keeps a file the user already has, a false positive deletes it.
- **What the sync engine deletes, for the record**: only `.part` intermediates, plus a *superseded pristine* copy after a clean rename - guarded on `is_update_clean` (never a locally-modified file) and a `normcase` comparison so it can never delete the file it just wrote. It never deletes on the strength of a Canvas-side diff.

## A silent swallow is only acceptable around something SMALL and cosmetic
The repo already fixed this once - `sync/analysis.py:sync_progress_hook` raised `NameError` on every tick for months and the only symptom was a panel that never painted. The rule was applied there and nowhere else. A family scan (222 broad silent handlers; the size of the guarded body is what separates defensive code from a hiding place) found two more:
- **`sync_ui.py`'s Panopto `progress` hook swallowed 123 lines**, identical in shape to the documented bug. Now logs - but only the FIRST failure carries `exc_info`, with the rest counted via `_pan_hook_errs`. Unlike the analysis hook (once per course) this one fires per download/transcribe EVENT, so an unconditional traceback would bury the run's real errors under hundreds of identical stacks.
- **`_render_sync_history` degraded a failed read to `[]`**, which renders as *"you have no sync history"* - the same confusion this file already calls out for the hub read ("never mistake it for the user deleted everything").
- Left alone deliberately: `count_course_items` / `get_course_total_size_mb` (documented best-effort, the consequence is a wrong progress estimate), `_log_error`'s own handler (logging a logging failure is circular), and the query-param nav helpers (a malformed URL must not break the app, and a `finally` sets the critical flag).

## Do NOT put a stall watchdog on transcription - decided 2026-08-07
`panopto/stream.py`'s ffmpeg download gets one (see above) because the `.part` file grows continuously, so "no new bytes for 180s" is a reliable wedge signal. **Transcription is the opposite and must be left alone.** `transcribe()` emits a progress event per WHISPER SEGMENT, and before the first one there is a long silent stretch while faster-whisper loads (or downloads) a model that can be hundreds of MB. On a weak CPU with no GPU - the machine this was audited on - a watchdog generous enough not to kill a legitimately slow transcription is too generous to catch anything, and a tight one kills real work. The loop already polls `is_cancelled` every 0.3 s, so a user can always stop it; the residual risk is an unattended daily sync wedging, and that is the better trade. The worker's other failure modes ARE bounded: stderr goes to a temp FILE (a full pipe can never block the child), stdout is drained on a thread, a cancel does `kill()` + `wait(timeout=10)` before cleanup so the `.part` sidecars are not locked, and an exited-but-pipe-inherited worker is abandoned after a 3 s grace.

## A UI cancel is a `BaseException`, so cleanup after the loop is not cleanup (2026-08-09)
Found by the LIVE audit cancelling a real transcription, and it is the second round on this same `.part` sweep - the first round fixed the two exits it could see and left the one users actually take.
- **Clicking Cancel does not merely set a flag; it STOPS the script run.** Streamlit does that by raising `RerunException` / `StopException` from the next `st.*` call, which happens inside the `progress()` callback - i.e. *inside* the transcription loop. Both derive from `BaseException`, so nothing in `panopto/runner.py` caught them and they propagated **straight past the sweep**, which was ordinary statements after the loop carrying a comment claiming it covered "every route out of the phase". It covered every `break`; it covered no exception. The sweep is now in a **`finally`**, which is what that comment always meant.
- **The log is the tell, and its ABSENCE is the evidence.** On a normal cancel the phase writes "Transcription cancelled by user" or at least `transcribe_done`; on this one the debug log simply **ENDS** at `Download cancel event cleared`, mid-recording `[3/30]`. Nothing ran after the exception. Measured on course 43660: `<name>.txt.part` (8416 B) and `<name>.srt.part` (16536 B) left in the student's folder, **unlocked** - calling `_clean_part_files` by hand removed both instantly, which is what proves the sweep was never reached rather than having failed.
- **Why a leftover is worse than untidy**: `panopto/runner.py`'s own comment states the engine deliberately ignores `.part` artifacts everywhere - never healed onto a manifest row, never auto-discovered, never counted as study material - so *"nothing would ever remove or even mention them again"*.
- **The same cancel exposed a LATENT long-path bug, and it is the classic asymmetry**: every WRITE in `panopto/transcribe.py` goes through `make_long_path` (the sidecars are opened with it and committed with it) and the DELETE did not. That fails in the most misleading way available - on a default Windows install (`LongPathsEnabled = 0`, i.e. most users) a path over 260 chars raises ERROR_PATH_NOT_FOUND, Python surfaces it as **`FileNotFoundError`**, and the retry loop reads that as *"already gone"*: no removal, no retry, **no log**. The audit measured **259 paths over 255 characters** in that one course and the two abandoned sidecars were **341**. It did not bite on the dev box only because it has `LongPathsEnabled = 1` - the identical reason `office_safe_path`'s long-path bug survived. When a path-length fix "cannot be reproduced", check that registry key first.
- **The tests that were already there passed against BOTH defects**, because they anchored on `_clean_part_files(` appearing within **1800 characters** of `progress("transcribe_done")`. That is true of a sweep in a `finally`, of a sweep after the loop, and of a sweep that never runs - and documenting the fix pushed the call out of the window, so three of them then reported the sweep as *missing* when it was right there ("a brittle test anchor reads like a missing guard", again). They now resolve the sweep through the **AST** and assert the property that matters: that the call sits inside a `Try.finalbody`.
- `tests/test_transcribe_partial_cleanup.py`; 5 of 6 mutations caught, including one for each original defect. The survivor - `break` -> `continue` on the `FileNotFoundError` branch - is an **equivalent mutant**: on a genuinely absent file it spins six cheap iterations with `last_err` still `None`, so the outcome and the logging are unchanged.
- **The CRASH path does not sweep - only CANCEL does - and what contains that is a COUNT of one (measured on macOS 26.6.1, 2026-08-20).** Driving a real SIGSEGV into a live worker - which is exactly what an uncatchable native crash looks like, and the whole reason this runs out of process - confirmed containment works: the parent survived, raised `TranscriptionEngineCrash(exit_code=-11)`, faulthandler dumped the C-level traceback into the debug log, and the child was reaped. But `transcribe_in_subprocess` returned with **both `<name>.txt.part` and `<name>.srt.part` still on disk**; its sweep lives on the cancel branch, not in its own `finally`.
  - It is not user-visible today because there is **exactly ONE production caller** (`panopto/runner.py:_run_panopto_batch`) and its phase-level `finally` sweeps every task on the way out. So the guarantee rests entirely on the caller, and a second caller added anywhere would leak on the crash path with nothing failing - the same shape as `pdf_looks_real` landing on two of three delete sites and surviving eight months.
  - **Do NOT "fix" this by adding a sweep inside `transcribe_in_subprocess`'s own `finally`.** The runner retries a GPU crash on CPU by `continue`-ing to the SAME recording, and the engine-level function cannot tell a retry from an abandonment - sweeping there would delete work the retry is about to reuse the paths for. The phase is the boundary the tasks are already independent across, which is the same reasoning as `_run_phase` in post-processing and `_sec_category` in the secondary-content loop.
  - `test_every_caller_of_the_subprocess_runner_sweeps_in_a_finally` encodes the RULE rather than the count, so a legitimate new caller is fine provided it sweeps; `test_the_subprocess_runner_has_at_least_one_production_caller` stops it going vacuous at zero sites. `scripts/_mutate_transcribe_caller_sweep.py`, **4/4 caught** - and the mutant that justifies the test is the one older tests miss: a plausible `_retranscribe_one()` helper added outside the phase.

## An AppleScript string literal is escaped in ONE place, because the rule had three (2026-08-10)
Found by driving every macOS branch from Windows (`sys.platform`/`platform.system()` answered as darwin, real functions throughout - the same technique that caught the macOS Office delete-gate miss). An AppleScript string literal cannot span lines, so a raw `\n` **or `\r`** inside one is a SYNTAX error that kills the whole script; a bare `"` or `\` is an injection. Three places build such a literal, all three agreed on quotes and backslashes, and exactly one flattened only `\n`:

| builder | `\\` `"` | `\n` | `\r` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `engine/applescript_bridge._as_posix` | ok | ok | ok |
| `shared/helpers.native_folder_picker` | ok | ok | ok |
| `engine/notifications._show_macos_notification` | ok | ok | **missing** |

- **The one that was wrong takes a Canvas COURSE NAME.** `sync_ui.py` builds the daily-sync notification body as `f"{n} new files in {courses[0]['name']}."`, so a lone `\r` anywhere in a course name (or a user's pair label) reached the literal intact. Measured against the real function: the emitted script was `display notification "Body Lec\rture.doc" ...` - unterminated, rejected by osascript.
- **It fails SILENTLY, twice over.** The osascript call sits in a `try/except Exception` that logs at **debug** level, and it is fallback **#4 of 4** (UNUserNotificationCenter → NSUserNotification → pync → osascript), the first three passing the string as an object and so unaffected. So the notification simply never appears and nothing anywhere says why. That bounds the severity - no data loss, and only on a machine where the three PyObjC paths have already failed - but it is the *class* that matters, not this instance.
- **Same divergent-primitive shape as `make_long_path`'s duplicate in `core/sync_manager.py`**, and the same lesson: a rule written more than once is a rule some caller is following an old version of. `engine.applescript_bridge.applescript_string()` is now the single implementation and `_as_posix` is `applescript_string(path.resolve())`.
- **`applescript_bridge` is the right home because it imports nothing from the app at module level.** `shared/helpers.py` and `engine/notifications.py` both reach it, and it reaches `shared.helpers.get_config_dir` back - safe only because every one of those imports is function-scoped. A module-level app import here turns all three into a cycle, so the test checks the **level**, not merely the presence: a blanket ban would flag the existing lazy import and read as a violation where there is none.
- **Order is load-bearing**: backslash before quote. Quote-first produces `a\"b`, and escaping backslashes after that turns the escape itself into `a\\"b`, which closes the literal. A line break becomes a **space**, never `''` - deleting it would silently join two words of a name shown to the user.
- **The copies must be deleted, not merely bypassed.** A dormant `.replace('\\', '\\\\')` chain sitting beside the shared call is exactly how the rule drifts apart again, so a test rejects any `.replace()` of `\`, `"`, `\n` or `\r` inside those two functions.
- `tests/test_applescript_string_escaping.py` (17); all **8** mutations of the real code are caught, including each caller reverting to its own escaper - so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite. **Not reproducible from Windows at runtime** (`import subprocess` lives inside notifications.py's `if system == 'Darwin':` block, so off macOS the name is absent and path 4 dies on a `NameError` inside its own handler); the test injects it, and asserts the command was actually captured, because without that the check passes against a script that was never built.

## The Panopto **Shortcut** output: a link file is a first-class kind (2026-08-07)
`output_url` sits beside mp4/mp3/txt/srt as the FIRST toggle on the Section 4 card. It costs no bandwidth, no disk and no time, and it answers what a transcript cannot: take me back to the lecture, with the slides, the screen capture and Panopto's own search still attached. Framed as a companion, never a substitute - it is a pointer, so it dies with your Canvas/Panopto access, which is precisely when people want the offline copy.
- **The destination is frequently NOT free, and missing that shipped the feature as a silent no-op.** `core/canvas_logic.py:_create_link` already writes a `.url` for every Canvas **ExternalTool** module item - and a Panopto lecture IS an ExternalTool item named after the lecture. In the *match* layout the Panopto pass therefore computes a path Canvas has already filled. Measured on course 43660 in the real app: **36 recordings discovered, 34 identically-named `.url` files on disk, 0 shortcuts written, 0 manifest rows**, and a completion screen that mentioned Panopto not at all. Every unit test passed. **Overwriting is not the fix** - that file belongs to the Canvas file sync, which tracks its `content_sig` and would rewrite it next run, two subsystems clobbering one path for ever. `panopto/shortcut.py:resolve_shortcut_path` adopts a link WE produced wherever it sits, else takes the first free name, stepping over anything foreign - so the collision case lands at `<title> (Panopto).url` beside the Canvas one. The runner and `sync_plan._find` both ask that one function; computing it twice is how the writer and the analyzer stop agreeing, and the symptom is a link rewritten on every sync for ever.
- **`kind` is the extension for every other output and NOT for this one** (`.url` on Windows, `.webloc` on macOS). Everything goes through `kind_extension` / `kind_extensions` / `kind_from_path`. `make_recorder` in particular must not strip the suffix: a `.webloc` recorded as kind `webloc` answers a question nothing asks while the `url` row the analyzer wants reads as missing for ever. `kind_extensions` lists the other platform's suffix too, so a folder first synced on Windows is adopted on a Mac rather than duplicated.
- **A produced shortcut carries a source marker, and that marker is all that stands between it and `converters/url.py`**, which compiles every shortcut in a course folder into `Compiled_External_Links.txt` and then DELETES it. Without it the selected output is destroyed on every run and restored by the next sync, endlessly. The marker is a `[CanvasDownloader]` INI section (`.url`) or a sibling plist key (`.webloc`) - both ignored by the shell/Finder - and it lives in the file rather than in a manifest so it works in download mode, in sync mode, and in a folder whose manifest seed write failed. `shared/shortcuts.py` owns the format for all three consumers; `write_shortcut` strips newlines from the URL so Canvas-controlled data cannot open its own section and mark itself exempt.
- **A shortcut needs NO Panopto session, and that is the whole economy of the feature.** Discovery already performs the LTI launches, so `PanoptoVideo.panopto_host` is captured for free (`panopto/auth.py:extract_panopto_host`, whose host class excludes `/` - a lazy one matches from the FIRST scheme in a Canvas `retrieve?url=` link and yields a plausible-looking URL that points nowhere). The runner skips its per-course bootstrap entirely when nothing but links is wanted, so a course whose LTI chain is dead still gets its links. Verified live: 36 links, **0 runner handshakes**.
- **The Canvas module-item fallback is valid ONLY for `source == "module"`.** A `source="folder"` recording carries the item id of whichever module item's launch enumerated the folder - use it there and every recording in the course points at the SAME lecture, thirty shortcuts, one destination, no error anywhere.
- **Default OFF**, unlike mp3/txt/srt: the key did not exist before, so every stored contract answers False, and a True default would write a new file beside every recording in every folder the app has ever synced on the first run after an update.
- `panopto.settings.contract_from_ui_state` is now the ONE reader of the `persistent_pan_out_*` toggles - it replaced five hand-written copies (app.py, sync_ui.py x2, sync/analysis.py, ui/sync_dialogs.py), any one of which could have been missed with no symptom but a format the user selected never being produced.
- **macOS is the same feature in the other file format**, and every difference is downstream of one function (`shortcut_extension`). Verified by making `platform.system()` answer `"Darwin"` and driving the real runner: `.webloc` written, valid two-key plist (`URL` + the marker - Finder reads the first and ignores the second), recorded as kind `url`, the identical Canvas-link collision resolved to `<title> (Panopto).webloc`, the URL compiler's `*.webloc` glob sparing ours, and a Windows-synced folder's `.url` files adopted rather than duplicated. **What that cannot prove is Finder itself** - but `_create_link` has shipped the identical `plistlib.dumps({'URL': ...})` form on macOS for as long as external links have existed, so the only new thing on disk is one extra plist key. Nothing else is platform-gated: `make_long_path` returns its argument unchanged off Windows, and the mac spec bundles `assets/`, `shared/` and `panopto/` wholesale.
- Covered by `tests/test_panopto_shortcut.py`; all **27** mutations of the real code are caught (two of them macOS-only), so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite.

## The global Panopto switch: STORED contract vs EFFECTIVE contract (2026-08-09)
`panopto_globally_enabled` shipped as an almost entirely **cosmetic** setting. It had exactly two readers - `shared/legal.py` and the Settings dialog - and neither sat on an execution path, so switching Panopto off left Custom Download's Card 4 fetching every selected output, left three of the five Quick Download presets fetching recordings, and left the sync page running the **full discovery pass** and downloading. Only the Today daily sync honoured it, and only because `core/auto_sync.py` sets the run-scoped decline flag for an unrelated reason. Meanwhile `require_panopto_notice` logged *"Panopto skipped: turned off in Settings"* and **returned True so the run proceeded unchanged** - the log line was the only thing in the app that claimed a skip. Two pieces of shipped copy already specified the missing behaviour (`ui/auth.py`'s *"Off means: no institution lookup, no discovery, no acceptable-use dialog, and no recordings in any download or sync"*, and the toggle's own tooltip), so this was a bug whose spec was written in a comment directly above the control.
- **The fix is one named distinction, and it is the whole design.** A folder's `panopto_contract`, a preset's `pan_out_*` and Section 4's session toggles are all **STORED** - statements about what the user CONFIGURED, and what every display surface must keep reading. `panopto.settings.effective_contract(stored)` applies the switch and is what every execution entry point resolves through. Conflating the two is exactly why the switch had nowhere to land: `is_enabled(stored)` was the only gate.
- **Turning Panopto off is a statement about the FUTURE, never a rewrite of the past.** Nothing mutates: a folder's stored contract survives untouched and the user's Card 4 selections are not zeroed, so switching back on resumes recordings with the outputs originally chosen. `effective_contract` returns a value and performs **no write** - asserted with an `open`/`os.replace` spy, because a mutant that re-persisted the settings produced a **byte-identical file** and a content comparison saw nothing.
- **WHERE the gate goes is load-bearing, not a style choice - and the obvious placement is destructive.** The download-mode phase seeds each folder's `panopto_contract` (`app.py`, "Persist this run's contract") **before** it calls the batch, and `sync_ui` only ever seeds `if ... is None`, so a download run is the one thing that can overwrite it. A "one guard at the bottom, nothing can bypass it" gate inside `panopto/runner.py` would therefore let the phase start, write an **all-off contract over every folder it touched**, and turn a reversible preference into permanent per-folder data loss that nothing would restore. The gate is at `app.py:_next_phase_after_courses` (above the seed), `sync/analysis.py` (above discovery), and `sync_ui.py:_run_sync_panopto` as a **logged** backstop only.
- **It must NOT be folded into `compose_settings` or `is_enabled`.** `extract_contract` seeds from the first, so the switch reaching in there is the same data-loss bug by another route; the second is asked by DISPLAY code - the sync hub's stored config, the preset cards, and the ignored-recordings dialog's "which kinds count as configured" - so making it switch-aware reports that a folder set up for recordings never was.
- **In sync mode the switch skips the WORK, not just the result.** `sync/analysis.py` reads it directly into `_pan_on` before anything else: no stored-contract read, no manifest heal (which **writes**), no discovery. That is where the switch is worth the most - discovery is a per-recording LTI handshake, and at a university with no Panopto it is ~48 handshakes at 1-2s each that can only ever return nothing. `panopto_payload` stays `None`, already the documented shape for "the feature is disabled", so every consumer downstream needs no change.
- **`_pan = compose_settings(...)` moved INSIDE the gated branch**, so every read of it must be too - and this module swallows the whole Panopto block in a bare `except Exception`, so a stray read would surface as a logged warning and a silently absent payload, never a traceback. Same shape as the `isolate` bug in `core/canvas_logic.py`. Pinned by an AST test, not by reading.
- The two sync-page pair helpers short-circuit **before** their per-pair loop, which is both the honest answer and much cheaper: they run on every sync-page render and open a `SyncManager` per pair, so the switch replaces N SQLite connections with one config read.
- Covered by `tests/test_panopto_global_switch.py`; all **20** mutations of the real code are caught, so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite. Four of those mutants escaped a first, substring-based version of the tests purely because `from panopto.settings import is_globally_enabled` keeps the NAME in a function body after the guard using it is deleted - match the **call** via AST, and reject a test weakened into a compound (`if False and not ...`).

### The same switch, Pass 2: what the SURFACES do about it
**One rule decides every case: OFFER vs RECORD.** A surface that OFFERS Panopto (a control that would cause a recording to be fetched) goes unavailable. A surface that RECORDS it (a stored contract, a completion screen, sync history) is NEVER rewritten - it is a statement of fact about how something is configured, and that stays true while the switch is off - so it gets ANNOTATED instead. The seductive wrong turn is "the run skips it, so make the config viewer say *None selected*": that reports a folder set up for recordings as one that never was, and the pills reappear the moment the switch goes back on. Covered by `tests/test_panopto_switch_ui.py`; all **25** mutations caught.
- **Card 4 is DIMMED, never removed** (`ui/download_settings.py:_render_card_panopto_off`). Removing it leaves the download page with no mention of Panopto at all, so a user who switched it off weeks ago has no route back from the screen where they would look - the "a disabled control must say why" rule, whose whole point is that hiding the reason turns an unavailable thing into a dead end. It also keeps the big help card's "Card 4" section from documenting something absent. The off-state emits the **same three children as the collapsed on-state** (style / icon / header row) because Streamlit reconciles by position, and it keeps the header to ONE line because the row's geometry (`margin-top: -35px`, the icon at `top: -24px`) is tuned for one - the message rides in the TAG, reusing the on-state's own `OFF | <detail>` idiom. Two keys (`card_panopto` / `card_panopto_off`), the `stg_card_pan_off` trick, because a container carries no attributes; `st-key-card_panopto_off` still contains `st-key-card_panopto`, so the shared `[class*=]` card chrome applies unchanged.
- **Dimming is the app's ONE disabled recipe and nothing on top** - `brightness(0.5) saturate(0.5)`, plus `filter: none` on any disabled button INSIDE a dimmed card, because filter multiplies through nested elements and the shared `button[disabled]` rule would compound with the card's. A "50% opacity + 50% saturation" treatment was proposed and declined for exactly the reasons in "Disabled buttons: ONE recipe" - opacity multiplies, a second `filter` replaces.
- **The config viewers annotate once per COLUMN**, not per pill (`shared/components.py:render_config_summary_badges`): the pills stay, dimmed, under one "Won't run - Panopto is off in Settings" line. Five "won't run" badges say nothing five times. Nothing is annotated while the switch is on, or when the column has no outputs (that would put a notice on the majority of presets).
- **The skipped-recordings report is the THIRD member of an existing family**, not a new component - `shared/components.py:render_panopto_disabled_notice`, beside the size-skip and unpacked-archive panels, sharing `.skip-panel.skip-panel-solo` and its CSS verbatim. **It counts COURSES, not recordings, and that is forced by the fix**: switching Panopto off skips discovery, so the recording count is genuinely unknown and claiming one would mean running the very scan the switch avoids. The courses are resolved AT RENDER from each folder's stored contract (a cheap local read) - nothing is plumbed through the engine, so it cannot go stale.
  - **Its icon must be FILLED, and `fill-rule: evenodd` is a trap here.** The first film strip carried an inner `M8 6h8v12H8V6z` subpath, which under evenodd PUNCHES THE CENTRE OUT - the glyph became a hollow frame with a fraction of the ink of the solid funnel and archive box beside it, i.e. exactly the "reads as an element of a different family" failure the icon block's own comment warns about. Invisible in review and in the DOM; found by screenshotting the three panels together at **4x device scale** in the real gallery. Do that whenever adding a member.
- **Today gets its own line, never `skipped`** (`core/auto_sync.build_today_sync_notice` -> `panopto_off`). That field means "the folder is gone, go fix it"; this means "working exactly as you configured it". One needs an action, the other needs none, so merging them misreports both. It matters most here: the daily sync is the run nobody watches.
- **The transcription dialog stays reachable while a model or the CUDA libraries are installed**, relabelled "Manage installed models". It is the ONLY place either can be deleted and they run to multiple GB, so dimming it with them installed stranded that disk space behind a switch-on / delete / switch-off dance nobody discovers. "The engine is a SUBORDINATE of the master switch" was always about not offering to CONFIGURE a feature that cannot run - never about trapping the user's disk.
- `scripts/completion_gallery.py` carries a `d-panopto-off` variant rendering all three family panels together, which is how the icon defect above was found. The gallery runs with `--server.fileWatcherType none`, so **restart it after editing a module constant** - an edited SVG will otherwise render as the old one and read as "my fix did nothing".

### The transcription-setup notice is DISMISSIBLE on the sync list and nowhere else (2026-08-19)
The amber "Transcripts & Subtitles need a one-time setup" card re-rendered on **every** render of the sync page, for ever, with no way to close it. That is a fair report of a STANDING mismatch - the folder's stored `panopto_contract` asks for txt/srt and the next sync will silently not produce them - but every way out of it was heavy: install a model, switch Panopto off globally, or **re-download the whole course** with the outputs unticked. A folder's `panopto_contract` is written by the download flow (`app.py`) and seeded on first sync; the hub, the sync dialogs and the config viewer only READ it, so there is no UI that edits it. The user had no proportionate answer at all.
- **The fix is the FDA nudge's shape, not a plain close** (`shared/components.render_fda_nudge` is the precedent, twenty lines below in the same file): dismissing collapses the card to a one-line re-spawn link that is ALWAYS present. Dismissing therefore never HIDES the fact, it only stops it leading - the same rule that keeps Today's off-list footnote out of the Show-help-text gate. The flag is persisted (`panopto.settings.TX_NOTICE_DISMISSED_KEY`, top-level beside `panopto_globally_enabled`): session-only still nags once per launch, which is the same complaint.
- **`dismissible=` is per CALL SITE and the two differ on purpose.** The sync LIST notice is a standing property. The sync REVIEW notice is an EVENT report about the run in front of you ("N pending recordings are set to produce Transcript or Subtitle files"), seen once per analysis at the last checkpoint before the run starts - it keeps the full card whatever the stored flag says. A shared flag makes hiding it there easy and it is the worst place to hide it.
- **The stylesheet MOVED to `styles/global.css`, and that is a correctness requirement.** It was an inline `st.html(<style>)` inside the function, i.e. emitted only in the branch where the notice renders - and this notice now has a branch that flips **on a click, mid-session**, which is precisely the conditional-stylesheet bug (style hosts reconcile BY INDEX, so a branch flip shifts every LATER stylesheet onto its neighbour's host). Selectors are prefix-based (`st-key-tx_setup_card_` / `_btn_` / `_close_` / `_link_` / `_relink_`) so one rule covers both surfaces, and `inject_css()` puts them in the MAIN container where no fragment rerun can reach them. The button key changed from the bare `key` to `tx_setup_btn_{key}` so the prefix form is possible.
- **Both branches emit `_TX_NOTICE_SLOT_CHILDREN` (2) children**, padded with `pad_slot_children`, because they render at the same index and `addBlock` hands a new block the CHILDREN of whatever sat there. The collapsed slot sets `gap: 0` - a blank child is zero-HEIGHT, not zero-COST.
- Close and re-spawn are `on_click=` callbacks: this control sits directly above the Analyze / Quick Sync buttons on a long page, which is exactly where a double render's lost scroll anchor is felt.
- **`panopto/settings.py` grew a third writer, so the atomic write was extracted** into `_atomic_write_config(full, what)`. The READ deliberately stays inline in each writer - that is the half `tests/test_settings_coownership.py` asserts per writer, and folding it in would make that census stop measuring anything. The new writer is registered in both of that file's writer lists.
- **Verified in the REAL app** (isolated `CANVAS_DL_CONFIG_DIR`, the operator's own sync pair, 2026-08-19): card 864x128 -> link 864x19 -> re-spawned card 864x128, exactly 2 children in every state, 0 `stException`, 0 page errors, dismissal survived a full reload, and the live settings file was untouched.
- Covered by `tests/test_transcription_notice_dismissal.py` (24); all **20** mutations of the real code are caught (`scripts/_mutate_tx_notice.py`), so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite. One mutant escaped the first version: a CSS-coupling test that checked for the key as a SUBSTRING passed while the base paint rule alone had been renamed, because the `:hover` / `::before` rules still carried the old prefix. Extract the selectors and assert the exact base rule.
- **`scripts/_mutate_tx_notice.py` restores from an in-memory SNAPSHOT, never `git checkout`**, and ABORTS (restoring nothing) if a target changed underneath - this repo is routinely worked by two sessions at once. Its anchors are written with `\n` and translated to the file's own newline: these files are CRLF, and the first run reported a live guard as a stale anchor for exactly that reason.
- **`tests/test_mutation_anchors.py` now discovers every `scripts/_mutate_*.py`**, not just the office harness. It was hard-wired to one file while its own docstring said a new set "has to be covered without anyone remembering to extend this file". Positive control: breaking one anchor in the new harness fails it.

**What this does NOT fix, and it is the real gap**: the notice tells the user to do the one thing they may not want to do, and there is still no way to turn Transcript/Subtitles OFF for an existing folder short of re-downloading the course. Dismissal papers over that. If it is ever built, note that narrowing a folder's outputs is not the "one folder, one configuration" widening the product owner ruled against for `file_filter` - it is the opposite direction.

### A conditional `<style>` really does shift the EVENT host list - MEASURED, and three sites still had it (2026-08-20)
The rule above ("NEVER emit a `<style>` block CONDITIONALLY") was documented as Critical and **gated for the dialog variant only** (Rule 9). A whole-project sweep found **15 sites with the shape**; three were live and are fixed. Found by asking the self-critique question after the transcription notice landed - that change *armed* a latent instance, because its branch now flips on a click.
- **THE DIAGNOSTIC, and it is the reusable part**: sample `[data-testid="stEvent"] [data-testid="stHtml"]` in document order on every animation frame, then diff **by index** rather than by set. A missing stylesheet is invisible in a set comparison; a *displaced* one is the whole bug. Recorded across a real "Analyze, Review & Sync" on the sync page:

  | | |
  |---|---|
  | at rest | 5 hosts; `[2]` = the page's **277KB** main stylesheet, `[3]` hub button, `[4]` main-column buttons |
  | **t=+242ms** | `[2]` **REWRITTEN** with the sidebar's run-lock stylesheet; the 277KB sheet gone from the list entirely, while `[3]` and `[4]` still held the **previous screen's** stylesheets |
  | t=+288ms | settled |

  Index `[2]` held **three different stylesheets during one run**, index `[3]` three. After the fix the sidebar's sheets sit at fixed indices in every frame and no index is ever handed a stranger's sheet - the only remaining churn is a main-page sheet replaced by another main-page sheet when the screen genuinely changes, which is what reconciliation is for.
- **The event list is SMALL, so the blast radius is not "every stylesheet on the page"** - measured on the sync page: **5 hosts in `stEvent` against 10 style-bearing containers in `stMain`**, because `inject_css()` uses `st.markdown` (MAIN container) and only style-ONLY `st.html` is routed to the event root. Correct the instinct before sizing a fix.
- **All three sidebar guards were SELF-GATING, and that is what makes deleting them safe rather than a trade.** Every rule in the two lock blocks is scoped to `button:disabled`, and those buttons carry `disabled=_locked` / `disabled=_is_executing`; the active-nav block keys off `st-key-nav_btn_{mode}`, which matches nothing for a mode with no such button. So the `if` never changed what the page *looked* like - it only moved index boundaries, and **the sidebar renders BEFORE the main page**, so those boundaries sit ahead of every main-page sheet. Verified mid-run in the real app afterwards: Download/Today/logout dimmed at `brightness(0.5) saturate(0.5)` with `cursor: not-allowed` and the RUNNING mode's button at `filter: none` - the "you are here, and it's running" exclusion still fires - and the sidebar is pixel-identical at rest.
- **The course list was the fix landing on one of two twins.** `_render_multi_select_list` carries a comment saying its own `if combined_css:` was *"safe only by accident"*; `_render_single_select_list` still had it **and** nested the first row's `margin-top` inside the same guard - so a list whose courses carry no parenthetical code lost its `-10px` alignment *and* shifted the host list, in the same branch. Both now mirror each other exactly.
- **A test that guards one of two identical twins is the same defect one level up - and the mutation pass proved it on my own test.** After the fix the two renderers are textually identical from `boundary_css = []` onward, so the mutant's anchor matched **twice**, `replace(..., 1)` silently mutated the *multi*-select, and the run reported SURVIVED while the label said single-select. Anchor uniquely (include a preceding line) **and** parametrize the test over both twins.
- **`_is_style_only` is the ROUTING rule and it decides WHICH bug you have**, so classify before fixing: nothing but style tags and comments left over → the EVENT root container, one global index-addressed list (this class); one byte of real content beside the style tag → the MAIN container at the call site's own delta path, where a conditional emission is the **container-inheritance** hazard instead, local to its own parent. `ui/auth.py`'s `if display_user:` block is the second kind and is deliberately left alone.
- **12 further sites match the shape and were triaged, not fixed.** The question that separates them is *"can this branch flip while the rest of the page stays stable?"* - none of the twelve can; each either cannot flip in a session (the completion-card variants, the preset icon's base64 load) or flips together with a full screen replacement (sync history, sync review, hub layers). An audit **Rule 11** was considered and DEFERRED: it would need each of the twelve justified or suppressed, which is a separate piece of work with its own churn.
- Covered by `tests/test_conditional_stylesheets.py` (8); all **9** mutations caught (`scripts/_mutate_conditional_styles.py`), so re-run the mutation pass rather than just the suite.

### Two corrections to the transcription-notice entry above (2026-08-20)
- ~~**The sync REVIEW screen was never verified live**~~ - **VERIFIED 2026-08-20**, in the PACKAGED app on macOS 26.6.1 (WKWebView), against a real analysis of course 43660: 246 new files, **36 recordings**, and the card reads *"36 pending recordings are set to produce Transcript or Subtitle files."* - the `context_note` correct to the recording. Reaching it needs a pair whose folder contract asks for txt/srt pointed at a course with recordings; an EMPTY folder is what makes every recording actionable, which is what `_tx_recording_count` counts.
  - **The `dismissible=` split is confirmed on the one thing that could only be seen live**: with `transcription_setup_notice_dismissed: true` on disk, the sync LIST notice rendered as its one-line link while the sync REVIEW card rendered in FULL, with **no dismiss control at all**, in the same session against the same flag. That is the per-call-site design working, not two copies that happen to agree.
- **`ui/sync_review.py` carries an inline override for that card** (`margin-top: -24px`, keyed on the FULL key so it tucks under the metric row above it). The first version of the markup-to-CSS coupling test scanned `styles/global.css` only, so a key rename would have unbound that override silently. The test now scans every `styles/*.css` **and** every module mentioning the key prefix.

## A SENTINEL is not a measurement, and a display cell must never print one (2026-08-07)
`shared/helpers.check_disk_space` fails OPEN and reports **-1** for available/total when it cannot read the volume at all (invalid drive letter, disconnected share, any `OSError`). The Confirm Sync dialog multiplied that straight out and printed it: an unreachable target rendered **"Available Disk Space: -1048576 B"**. Reproduced against the real helper on `Q:
onexistent`.
- **`format_file_size` is a DISPLAY CELL** in exactly the sense `engine/progress_dashboard.py` means, and is bound by the same rule that module already states ("nothing a counter can hold may raise or render as a non-number"). It is simply the one such cell that lives OUTSIDE that module - in `core/sync_manager.py` - so the hardening pass there never reached it, while **18 render sites** call it, several inside an `@st.dialog` where a raised exception blanks the modal. It now coerces non-numeric/NaN/inf to 0 and **clamps negatives**, so it can never emit a negative byte count whatever a subsystem hands it.
- **The `None` half is not hypothetical**: Canvas sizes are read as `getattr(file_obj, 'size', 0)`, and that default applies only when the attribute is ABSENT - present-and-null yields `None`. `core/canvas_logic.py` writes `... or 0` at ONE `original_size=` site and not at the four others, so the codebase is already split on whether that happens, and a `None` reaching a manifest row comes back out of it into `ui/sync_dialogs.py`'s `format_file_size(f.original_size)`.
- **"0 B" is NOT the fix for the sentinel and must never be substituted** - it reads as a completely full disk, which is wrong in the opposite direction and would make a user cancel a sync that had plenty of room. `shared/helpers.format_available_space()` is the ONE renderer that says **"Unknown"**, and it lives beside `check_disk_space` because the sentinel is that function's convention (`DISK_SPACE_UNKNOWN`). A genuine zero still formats as "0 B" - the two mean opposite things and must not collapse.
- **The first version of the test re-implemented the dialog's expression instead of calling it**, and two mutations survived because of it - the same "verify the REAL thing, not a copy" trap this file already documents. Extracting the decision into one named function is what made it testable. `tests/test_file_size_display.py`; all **11** mutations caught.

## A declined extraction must leave NOTHING behind (2026-08-07)
`converters/archive.extract_archive` creates the target folder **before** it can read the archive's member list, so every guard that trips afterwards - the zip-bomb ratio check, the zip-slip / tar traversal blocks, a corrupt archive - left an empty directory sitting next to the untouched archive. `_decline()` already existed for exactly this on the `max_files` path and says so in its own docstring; the exception path simply never called it. Now it does.
- **`_decline` uses `os.rmdir`, which removes ONLY an empty directory** - that is what makes this safe for a PARTIAL extraction. Anything already written keeps the folder, and the folder stays; a test drives a mid-extraction `OSError` to prove it.
- Do not "improve" it to `shutil.rmtree`: that would discard files a half-finished extraction had already produced, turning a recoverable failure into data loss. `tests/test_archive_decline_cleanup.py`; all **5** mutations caught, including the rmtree one.

## Never destroy a LIVE session's processes on the strength of `clean_exit` (2026-08-07)
`core/health_log._reap_recorded_orphans` kills the child processes the previous session recorded. Its identity proof (pid + image name + creation time, plus the WebView2 `--user-data-dir`) is sound, but it was answering the wrong question first: **`clean_exit: False` does not mean "that session died"** - it is what the state file says for the whole time a session is RUNNING (`_save_state` writes it on every sample; only `session_end` clears it).
- So a second instance booting beside a live first one reads the first one's CURRENT children and kills them: the running window's **WebView2 process**, or the **ffmpeg / transcription worker of a sync in flight**. Reproduced directly against the real function - a live child recorded, reaped and dead in one call.
- `start.py`'s single-instance guard normally prevents a second instance, but it **fails OPEN by design** in three ways (mutex creation failure, the `CANVAS_DL_ALLOW_MULTI` escape hatch, any exception on the flock path), and its Windows mutex is **session-local** - two Terminal Services sessions for one user share `%APPDATA%` but not the mutex. A reaper that can destroy a live session must not rest on a guard that is allowed to let a second instance through.
- **The guard is LIVENESS, and it is the only thing that changed.** The per-child identity proof was never the weak part - it cannot help here, because for a live session those children genuinely DO match. The cost of skipping a reap is one leaked process the next clean launch collects; the cost of a wrong kill is a user's window vanishing mid-sync.
- **A missing/unreadable pid FALLS THROUGH to the identity proofs - refusing there was tried first and reversed on evidence.** Refusing looks safer and is not, for two reasons found by running the existing suite: `_save_state` has written `pid` beside `children` since this module's first commit, so a record with children and no pid **cannot** have come from a live session (the only thing this guard defends against); and four tests in `tests/test_health_log.py` pass no pid, three of which assert a spare - so refusing would have made them **vacuous**, passing with the identity proofs deleted. A guard that disables the tests protecting the code around it is not a safe guard.
- `tests/test_orphan_reaper_liveness.py` covers both directions (a live session's child is spared, a genuinely dead session's orphan is still reaped) and pins the pre-existing recycled-pid and image-name proofs. 7 of 8 mutations caught across both files; the eighth (`_prev_pid = 0` -> `1` in the unreadable branch) is an **equivalent mutant on Windows**, where pid 1 does not exist, so it falls through exactly as the real code does.

## `open(path, 'w')` TRUNCATES first - the settings file was written five ways
`ui/auth.py` persists `canvas_downloader_settings.json` in FIVE places. Three did tmp + fsync + `os.replace`; the two that did not were the legacy token-migration paths in `restore_saved_session`, which run at **startup**, on the very run that moves a token out of the JSON and into the keyring - i.e. exactly when the file is most worth not corrupting. A crash or a full disk between the truncate and the write leaves the user's settings empty. `write_config_atomically(config)` is now the one helper; it returns False rather than raising, because a settings write must not be able to abort a login. The three inline copies are correct and were deliberately left alone (one carries bespoke amber-notice error handling) - the test asserts the INVARIANT ("no write to CONFIG_FILE truncates in place"), so it covers all five regardless of form.

## Secondary content: seven independent categories, and work already IN FLIGHT
`_download_secondary_content` ran its seven `_fetch_and_save_*` categories as seven bare calls in a row. A malformed quiz says nothing about the user's submissions, but the first category to raise took out two things:
- **every category after it**, silently - the user asked for quizzes + submissions + rubrics and got one generic "Canvas Content Error" with no way to tell which parts ran (the outer handler in `_canvas_content_phase` catches it, so this was never a crash - just missing content);
- **the `asyncio.gather` at the end**, which is what awaits the attachment tasks assignments/announcements have already `create_task`'d. Skipping it means `asyncio.run` cancels them at loop close and those files silently never arrive.
`_sec_category(label, fn, *args)` isolates each one and names it in the error; the gather moved into a `finally`, because work already scheduled must survive a failure in a LATER category. Same principle as `_run_phase` in post-processing: **guard at the boundary the siblings are already independent across.**

## The `_NewVersion` redirect must be COMMITTED before it is announced
`_download_file_async` diverted an edited local file to `<stem>_NewVersion<ext>`, then announced it via `progress_callback`, and only THEN assigned `filepath = _diverted`. Anything the callback raised therefore skipped the assignment, left `filepath` pointing at the user's edited copy, and the download below overwrote it - with the enclosing handler a bare `pass`, so nothing recorded that the protection had been bypassed. A UI callback is precisely the kind of thing that raises. **Commit to the safe path first; the cosmetic step follows.** Reordering means `filename` is already the new name when the message is built, so the original is captured in `_original_name` - the sentence is about the file the user edited, not the one being written. Note the INNER handler was always correct (`_pristine = False` on failure → divert), which is what made the outer one easy to miss.

## `import x` inside a function makes `x` LOCAL for the whole function
A reference above that import raises `UnboundLocalError` - and because an enclosing `except Exception` catches it, the symptom is a wrong log message and a silently skipped branch, not a traceback. This audit *introduced* one in `converters/archive.py` (module used `logging` only inside its `except` handler; a new reference above it activated the trap) and caught it only by running the real function - the observed error was `cannot access local variable 'logging'`, on a path where the outer handler then reported "Failed to extract" for an extraction that had actually succeeded. Same shape as the `isolate` bug in `core/canvas_logic.py`. `tests/test_failure_hardening.py` now scans every function for it; **the scanner must take `min()` over the import lines** (`ast.walk` is breadth-first, so the first import it meets is not the earliest in the source) **and skip annotations** (a `-> Path` return annotation is evaluated at def-time in the ENCLOSING scope, so it is never shadowed). Both flaws produced false positives on the first pass.

## An Office converter deletes the user's original - so the PDF must be PROVEN first
Every Office converter ends by deleting the file it converted FROM. That is intended (the PDF replaces the legacy `.doc`/`.xls`/`.ppt`) and it is the one irreversible step in the pipeline, on a file that may be the user's only copy. **Word and Excel were doing it on the strength of "the COM call did not raise"** - and Office does not always raise: `SaveAs` / `ExportAsFixedFormat` can return normally having written nothing (a protected or repaired document, a locked-down `AutomationSecurity` policy, or for Excel a broken printer driver, which its PDF export goes through). PowerPoint tested `exists()`, which still passes a 0-byte stub. `converters/verify.py:pdf_looks_real` is the shared gate (present, non-empty, `%PDF` magic, not truncated) and all three call it BEFORE the delete; the tests assert the ordering, not mere presence.

**That fix landed on ONE PLATFORM ONLY, and the miss survived until 2026-08-08.** Each converter has TWO delete sites - the Windows/COM branch and the macOS/AppleScript branch - and only the COM one got the gate. The macOS branch deleted the original on `run_applescript` returning True, whose success test is **`dst.exists()`** - which is *precisely* the check the paragraph above records as too weak ("PowerPoint tested `exists()`, which is better but still passes a 0-byte stub"). So on macOS the very same stub that motivated this section still destroyed the user's only copy of a `.doc`/`.xls`/`.ppt`. Driven down the real macOS branch (by making `sys.platform` report `darwin` and letting the bridge leave a stub): before, all three deleted the original for a 0-byte, a truncated and a non-PDF output; after, all three keep it and report why, while a genuine PDF still replaces the source. **The lesson is the counting one**: a converter with two delete sites needs two gates, and `tests/test_crash_vector_hardening.py` now asserts `len(pdf_looks_real) >= len(source unlinks)` per file rather than "a gate exists".

## An artifact LINK is not a delivery - hand over a FILE (2026-08-15)
Every `claude.ai/code/artifact/<uuid>` link handed to this operator has 404'd in their browser - not once, but on all five artifacts published since 2026-07-23. The document was written, the operator could not read a word of it, and the session ended believing it had delivered.
- **The publish is NOT the failure, and proving that is the whole diagnosis.** Two checks separate "publishing is broken" from "viewing is broken", and they take seconds: `Artifact action:"list"` (the record exists, `(mine)`, correct title, correct URL) and `WebFetch` on that URL (returns the full HTML, *"owned by you, private"*). Both passed while the browser showed **Page not found**. So the artifact is real, intact, and owned by `mr.fireworkguydk@gmail.com` - the 404 is the *viewing context* failing to authenticate as that owner, and nothing a future session writes into the HTML can change it.
- **Do not attempt to fix it by rewriting the artifact.** A malformed page renders broken; it does not 404. A 404 at the URL is routing or auth, one layer above anything the content controls. Regenerating, re-titling or re-publishing costs a turn and changes nothing.
- **THE RULE: whenever a deliverable is published as an artifact, ALSO write it as a standalone `.html` file on disk and give the absolute path.** The artifact link is a bonus, never the handoff. Volunteer the path in the same message - do not wait to be told the link is dead, because the operator has no way to know whether the 404 is their end or a document that was never written.
- **The source file is NOT a valid standalone page, and copying it verbatim ships a subtly wrong document.** The Artifact tool wraps the file in `<!doctype html><html><head>…</head><body>` at publish time, so an artifact source correctly begins at `<title>`/`<style>` with no doctype. Open that same file with `file://` and the missing doctype puts the browser in **QUIRKS MODE** - a different box model, so the layout the artifact was designed and reviewed in is not the layout the operator sees. Wrap before handing over: doctype, `<html lang>`, `<meta charset="utf-8">`, `<meta name="viewport">`, the `<title>` moved into `<head>`. Verify the result starts with `<!doctype html>` and holds zero external `http(s)` references, then it opens anywhere with no server.
- **Where to put it**: alongside the work it belongs to, in a purpose-named folder (`video/walkthrough-script.html` for the launch walkthrough), and say plainly that it is untracked so the operator can delete or gitignore it. Never leave the only copy in the session scratchpad - that is temp, session-scoped, and gone by the next conversation.
- **The one test that tells the operator which half is broken**, if they want to recover artifact links at all: open `claude.ai/code/artifacts` (the gallery) in the same browser. Gallery loads and lists the document -> the direct link is the problem, open it from the gallery. Gallery also 404s or is empty -> that browser session is not signed in as the artifact's owner account, which is the reading this evidence favours. Either way it is an account/surface question on their side, so **it is not a blocker for delivering the work** - the file on disk already did that.

## Auditing this app: two playbooks, and the one input that matters
- **THIS FILE IS THE CROSS-MACHINE MEMORY, and that is why it is enormous.** The operator works from at least three checkouts (laptop, main desktop, and a rented macOS audit box) and any per-session assistant memory lives OUTSIDE the repo, so it does not travel and does not survive a machine being reimaged - which the audit box periodically is. Anything durable therefore belongs here, in `tests/audit/AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`, `tests/audit/MAC_RUNBOOK.md` or `AUDIT_PLAYBOOK.md`, written mechanism-first (what breaks, the measurement, why the obvious fix is wrong) rather than as a summary. Assistant memory is for facts about the PERSON and the working relationship; anything the repo should own goes in the repo, and goes in the same commit as the fix.
- **Marketing, SEO and the launch live in `marketing/`, not here.** Same rule and same reason as the audit documents above: a website change, a search-visibility finding or a positioning decision is durable, so it belongs in the repo and travels between machines. `marketing/README.md` is the index; **`FINDINGS.md`** is the register (every defect found, its evidence, its status, and why anything deferred was deferred); **`STRATEGY.md`** holds the SETTLED decisions; **`SITE_RUNBOOK.md`** is what to read before touching `docs/`; **`PLAYBOOK.md`** is the off-site plan; **`CHANGELOG.md`** records what exists now. Four facts from it that bite ENGINEERING and not just copy: (a) the website must advertise the newest shipped **TAG**, never `version.py`, which `tests/test_version_leads_tags.py` deliberately keeps AHEAD of every tag - the homepage advertised a 2.0.2 nobody could download; (b) a `<button>` that receives a scripted `.href` is a **dead control**, and that shipped - both download buttons on `/releases.html` did nothing for six days after three `<a href="#">` were converted to buttons and only one got a handler; (c) `preload="none"` is **ignored while `autoplay` is present**, so deferring a video means withholding the `src`, which took mobile LCP from 5300 ms to 2764 ms; (d) student-facing copy says **"Canvas", never "LMS"** - a product-owner ruling that overrides SEO instinct and applies to app strings too, not only the website.

- **`AUDIT_PLAYBOOK.md`** (repo root) - the OFFLINE crash/data-loss audit: the six techniques ranked by what they actually returned, the twelve sweeps that came back clean and must not be repeated, the reproduce→fix→test→mutate→verify workflow, and the mutation-harness hazards that cost this session two wasted passes.
- **`tests/audit/README.md` + `RUNBOOK.md`**, driven by **`.claude/skills/audit-live/SKILL.md`** - the LIVE audit: the real app, a real browser, real Canvas, five oracles.
- **The primary input to an offline audit is THIS FILE.** Every finding of the 2026-08-08 sweep was a class already documented here, surviving in a module the original fix did not reach. So the productive question is never "what could be wrong?" but **"which module did the last fix for this class NOT reach?"**
- **`.claude/skills/` is deliberately UN-ignored** in `.gitignore` (`.claude/*` + `!.claude/skills/`). `tests/test_audit_docs_match_cli.py` asserts the existence *and content* of the audit skill, so ignoring the whole tree made four tests unpassable on a fresh clone while presenting as a missing file rather than a packaging mistake. Note `.claude/*` and not `.claude/` - git will not descend into an excluded DIRECTORY, so a negation inside one can never re-include it.
- **The macOS audit has its own four files** (2026-08-10), because it runs on a RENTED machine where a wrong command costs money: `tests/audit/MAC_AUDIT_GUIDE.md` (renting, remote access, what to do the night before), `MAC_RUNBOOK.md` (the phase order and the traps), `MAC_AUDIT_PROMPT.md` (the agent's brief), plus `scripts/mac_audit_bootstrap.sh` (bare macOS → ready, idempotent because it is run once per OS install), `scripts/mac_audit_doctor.py` (preflight; refuses to pass outside an Aqua session) and `scripts/mac_smoke.py` (every macOS check that needs no human eye). Guarded by `tests/test_mac_audit_tooling.py`.
- **A documented command must satisfy its required POSITIONALS and required FLAGS, not just have real flag names.** `tests/test_audit_docs_match_cli.py` originally checked only that the flags *present* existed - which cannot notice an omitted one. Four real defects were hiding behind that: `finding add` (positional `title`), `flow download` (positional `name`), `seed apply` (positional `folder`) and `check download` (positional `folder` **and** required `--expect`). Every one of them is copy-pasted verbatim by a session that then loses time to an argparse error. The extractor also has to JOIN backslash continuations first - without that a correct multi-line command reads as missing everything after line one.

## Never run a mutation pass on a tree something might COMMIT (2026-08-10)
**The thing that commits is usually a SECOND CLAUDE SESSION, not a background job** (recorded 2026-08-11, after it was stated mid-audit: *"just letting you know a parallel session is editing right now aswell."*). This operator routinely runs more than one session against the SAME checkout - `/Users/m1/Canvas_Downloader`, not separate worktrees - and that changes three things:
- **The mutation window is not yours to control.** The measured incident below reads as a warning about background jobs; the realistic cause is another agent committing while your mutant is on disk.
- **Shared documents collide.** `CLAUDE.md`, `tests/audit/AUDIT_FINDINGS.md` and the runbooks are exactly what two sessions both want to append to, and all are large enough that a clobber is invisible afterwards. If another session is live, do the code and the tests and HAND the operator the prose rather than appending it yourself.
- **`git status` is not a picture of YOUR work.** A session-start snapshot said "clean" while six files were modified by the time it was read. Never read unexpected modifications as stale work to tidy up, and never `git add -A` blind - scope every git command to your own paths.
Check before a mutation pass and before a shared-doc write. If a pass already ran, `git log --since` for commits inside the window and re-run the FULL suite, not just the targeted file.

A mutation pass deliberately writes broken code into the working tree and restores it seconds later. A commit that lands inside that window captures the mutant, and the script's own `restore()` reports success **truthfully** - it simply ran after the snapshot was already taken.
- **Measured**: `panopto/shortcut.py` was committed with `kind_extensions` returning only the native suffix, so a folder synced on Windows would be re-produced under a second name on macOS instead of adopted - cross-platform shortcut adoption silently broken in HEAD, in the exact subsystem being prepared for a macOS audit.
- **The full suite is the detector, and only the full suite.** The targeted file passed; three `test_panopto_shortcut.py` tests - in a file the mutation pass never named - are what exposed it. **Re-run the whole suite after every mutation pass.**
- **The same commit captured a test's temp file.** A guard-on-the-guard wrote `_macos_branch_probe_tmp.py` into the REPO ROOT for the few milliseconds an assertion took. A test must never put a file where a concurrent `git add -A` can see it: give the scanner a root parameter and point the test at `tmp_path`.
- Also settled: a targeted mutation run (`pytest <file> -x -k <subset>`) costs **0.9 s** against ~75 s for the whole file, because the app-graph import dominates. If a targeted run cannot catch a mutant, no amount of unrelated tests would have.

### A recorded mutation score decays SILENTLY, because the anchors are literal strings (2026-08-13)
`scripts/_mutate_office_guard.py` mutates by exact string replacement, so a mutant whose `old` text has moved cannot run at all - and the score written down when it was last run keeps being quoted.
- **Measured**: `f6caf04` wrote the concurrency set and recorded **8/9**; `70ce78c` then inserted `_note_office_preexisting(app_name)` between `with _office_app_lock(app_name):` and `return _run_applescript_locked(`, invalidating the anchor for *"run_applescript stops taking the lock"*. From that commit the mutant could not run on any platform, while `tests/audit/MAC_OFFICE_FIXES.md` still said 8/9. The quit set WAS re-anchored in the same window (`7a2a674`) - so this rots one set at a time, and only whoever re-runs the pass would see it.
- **Re-anchored by hand and run: CAUGHT.** So the protection was real (`test_run_applescript_takes_the_lock` asserts the `with` through the AST); only the harness had gone stale. **Distinguish those two outcomes before concluding anything** - "anchor missing" is not "untested", it is "unmeasured".
- **`tests/test_mutation_anchors.py` makes staleness fail the SUITE**, in the same commit that moves the code, rather than at the next deliberate mutation pass. It discovers `*_MUTANTS` sets by NAME so a new set is covered without anyone extending it, checks each `old` still resolves, that each mutant actually changes the file, and that every `*_TEST` path exists (a set pointed at a deleted test file reports every mutant as *caught*, because pytest exits non-zero on a missing path). It has a positive control: breaking one anchor makes it fail.
- **It is NOT a substitute for the pass.** An anchor can resolve and the mutant still survive. It only guarantees that a recorded score describes mutants that could actually run.
- **Windows will report survivors that macOS catches, and that is not a regression.** Three of the concurrency mutants are covered only by `skipif(sys.platform != "darwin")` tests (the flock path), so a Windows pass reads 5/9 where macOS reads 8/9. Check *which* survivors before treating a number as a finding.
- **Writing a control for this needs the file tools, not a heredoc**: the anchors are full of literal `\n` two-character sequences and this shell mangles backslashes - the first version of the control silently replaced nothing and "passed", which is exactly the failure it exists to catch. That hazard is already recorded under the institution picker; it applies to every anchor-shaped test.

### The mutation window DESTROYS work too, and that direction is the silent one (2026-08-13)
The recorded hazard is a commit landing mid-pass and CAPTURING a mutant. The mirror is worse, because nothing reports it: `_clean()` runs ONCE at the start and restore was a hard `git checkout --`, so **an edit made to a target while the pass is running was restored away with no message**, and the pass went on reporting truthfully afterwards. Measured by walking into it: a docstring written into `engine/applescript_bridge.py` during a background pass, gone - noticed only because the editor warned the file had changed underneath it.
