Two of them read the raw `filename` where the engine writes **`preferred_disk_name`**, which APPENDS the real extension when the curated display name lacks it - so a file shown as "Lecture 1" and stored as "Lecture 1.pdf" was counted one way and downloaded another. Same divergent-primitive shape as `make_long_path`'s duplicate and the three AppleScript escapers.
- **Scope is enforced ONCE, in `analyze_course`** - the only place both the Review and Quick Sync flows pass through, so they cannot disagree and one contract read decides. Quick Sync's `apply_file_filter` is **deleted**, not kept as a backstop: two enforcement points each reading the contract independently is exactly how the six-site version got one site wrong.
- **The gate is `real_canvas_file_id`, NOT `id > 0`.** It is already this app's answer to "is this a real Canvas FILE?", and it says yes for the `attachment` band - which an isolate-mode attachment arrives under in the ANALYZER while the engine downloads it under its real positive id and filters it there. Gating on the sign leaves every out-of-scope attachment permanently new. Every other synthetic band is an ENTITY (assignment, quiz, announcement, …) governed by the **secondary-content contract, which `_canvas_content_phase` does NOT gate on `file_filter`** - so scoping those by extension recreates the identical bug in mirror image, for `.html` exports. `sync/execution.py`'s size gate independently hand-rolls the same predicate (`_gate_id > 0 or secondary_id_type(...) == 'attachment'`), which is corroboration that this is the engine's own notion of a real file.
- **CUSTODY OUTRANKS SCOPE.** The rule answers *"may this folder GAIN this file?"*, so a row with a `downloaded_at` keeps its tracking whatever its extension - reachable by a Canvas-side rename, which keeps the id and can change the suffix. Dropping it would silently stop reporting updates for a file the user holds.
- **The reclassification is a QUERY, not a migration.** `get_ignored_files` asks the folder's own contract, so rows written before this version are covered for free, the answer follows the folder instead of being frozen at whatever moment a migration ran, and there is no irreversible write to get wrong. Nothing is rewritten - a test asserts the row survives untouched and only its REPORTING changed. A damaged contract falls back to `'all'`, i.e. lists everything: hiding a decision the user really made is the unrecoverable direction.
- **`file_in_scope` must URL-unquote, because `_sanitize_filename` does.** The engine asks about the SANITIZED name and the analyzer plus both estimators ask about the raw one. Measured: `Lecture%2Epdf` sanitizes to `Lecture.pdf`, so the engine downloads it while an un-unquoted check saw no extension and dropped it - **the silent-missing-file direction, worse than the bug the predicate exists to fix**. Canvas serves URL-encoded filenames routinely (this file already records `'Klyngevejledning+-+Upload.pptx'`). Unquoting inside the predicate rather than at the six call sites is what makes them agree by construction; none of the sanitizer's other steps can change a short extension.
- **`.pdf` is the discriminator that separates this allowlist from the PowerPoint CONVERTER's input set.** Five other sites list `.ppt/.pptx/.pptm/.pot/.potx` WITHOUT `.pdf` (`converters/post_processing.py`, `engine/applescript_bridge.py`, `shared/helpers.py`, `sync/execution.py` x2) - a different concept that must never be merged, because a PDF is not converted to PDF. The single-definition test requires all three of `.pptm`, `.potx` and `.pdf`, and still caught the genuine fifth copy in `app.py`.
- Covered by `tests/test_folder_scope.py` (70); all **22** mutations caught.

### A name a function READS that nothing BINDS is a guaranteed crash - and the Pages fix shipped one
The 76-file fix added `module_item_in_scope(item.type, file_filter)` to `_get_files_from_modules` - where **`file_filter` is not a parameter of that method, is never assigned in it, and is not a module global**. A `NameError` on every module Page or link, in the metadata scan that feeds the sync analyzer, i.e. on most real courses. **The whole suite passed**, because every test of that fix asserted source SHAPE and the shape was right; the browser harness passed a file list straight to `analyze_course` and never called the scan.
- Same class as the `isolate` bug this file already documents, and the second time it has cost a release gate. `tests/test_unbound_names.py` now asserts the property across the whole tree: for every function, every name it loads is bound by that function, an enclosing function, the module, or builtins. **The defect itself is its positive control** - a scanner that reports 0 on a fixed tree proves nothing.
- Deliberately narrow: it catches "bound NOWHERE reachable" (`NameError`), not "bound only on some paths" (`UnboundLocalError` - the `isolate` bug's other half), because deciding reachability in general needs flow analysis and would misfire on ordinary early-return code. A module with a star import is skipped rather than guessed at.
- The false-positive set is the interesting part and is all parametrised: closures, a global defined AFTER its reader, `global`/`nonlocal`, comprehension and lambda targets, walrus, `with as`, `except as`, `*args/**kwargs`, function-scoped imports (this repo's cycle-breaker), match captures, and a method reaching a class attribute through `self`. A class body's names are NOT visible to its methods, so methods are visited with the ENCLOSING scope, not the class's.

### The "not a complete copy" notice: two surfaces, one text (2026-08-11)
Asked for by the product owner in the same breath as the ruling. `render_folder_scope_notice` is the **fourth** member of the "deliberately left alone" panel family on both completion screens; `render_folder_scope_review_notice` states the same fact on the sync review screen as an info notice.
- **Two presentations, ONE copy** (`folder_scope_copy`). The review page does not inject `completion.css` and must not start: that file's ghost-box purge rule is the **live** one (61 nodes, genuinely collapsing) and the completion screens are tuned WITH the collapse, so injecting it there re-spaces the review screen. So the surfaces own their markup and share their words - writing the copy twice is how the two would come to describe the folder differently, which is the exact failure this whole change is about.
- **`folder_scope_copy` takes an `int`, not the rows.** Both return values are interpolated into HTML unescaped (they carry `<b>` deliberately), so the only safe signature is one that CANNOT receive a course name. With an int in, every character is a literal from that file and the Rule 4 suppression is a statement of fact rather than a promise. Course names are escaped where they enter, in the callers. The suppression must be **inline** - the audit reads the flagged line and the one directly above it, so a three-line comment block above does not reach.
- **It replaced an amber Quick-Sync line that told users to widen their folder**: "N files outside this course's file-type filter", whose detail said to "run a normal Analyze, Review & Sync and select them manually" - an instruction the analyzer now correctly declines to make possible. It was also a per-run event report for a STANDING property, in one flow out of two. The `filtered` tally is gone from `qs_skipped` because it is now structurally always zero, and a counter that can only be zero reads as live information.
- **NO WIDENING CONTROL** (decided 2026-08-11): one folder, one configuration. The notice names the one route that does work - downloading the course again with All Files, which rewrites the folder's contract - instead of a button that would leave the folder in a shape nothing else in the app can describe.
- **The count is out-of-scope FILES, and 0 means "not measured", never "none".** A plain download never asks Canvas which files it is declining, so only the sync path can render the per-course badge. Pages and links are described qualitatively because they are dropped at the metadata scan, upstream of the analyzer that does the counting.
- **The glyph's WEIGHT is measured, and the first version failed exactly as the film strip did.** Counting glyph-coloured pixels at 4x device scale in the real gallery: funnel **34.6%**, archive box **50.4%**, and 2.4px-tall bars **14.3%** - under half its lightest sibling, so it read as line art beside two solid shapes. At 4.5px the three bars measure **29.8%**, i.e. the funnel's weight, which is the right sibling to match because both name the FILTER rather than the thing filtered. Three decreasing bars, not a folder (too close to the archive box), not a document (that is the "files downloaded" metric card's icon two rows above), not a pie (reads as progress on a completion screen).
- Verified in the browser before and after on every surface: the review notice sits 18px below the metric row it qualifies and 30px above the next section, with its left and right edges exactly on the metric row's; `?scope=0` (All Files) is the silence control and shows nothing.

### The sync completion screen rendered its "left alone" panels TWICE
`show_sync_complete` called `render_archives_skipped_notice` and `render_panopto_disabled_notice` in **both** of its notice blocks - at the same level inside its single `with fresh_container(...)`, with no branch and no `return` between them, so both ran every time. Driving the real function produced **4** `.skip-panel`s for 2 facts.
- **It shipped unseen for two reasons worth remembering.** Both conditions are uncommon (`pp_archives_skipped`, "Panopto is off"), so most screens had nothing to double - and **`scripts/completion_gallery.py`, the review instrument for these screens, mirrors `app.py`'s single block and was therefore MORE correct than the app**. When a gallery and the app disagree, the gallery is evidence, not automatically the mock that is wrong.
- **A test asserted the duplication as intended** ("sync/completion.py has two terminal paths and both need the notice"). There is one: `show_sync_complete` is the only function there that renders a completion card. Three independent proofs settled it - the AST shows a flat statement sequence, the browser shows 4 panels, and `app.py`'s comment already said the order is "the same on both completion screens" while having one of each. The count is now pinned for all three family members, on both screens.
- The panels stay in the EXPANDER group (before the error section, which is last because it is the one the user opens); only the duplicate in the NOTICES group was removed.

## The pre-release audit of the UNTOUCHED corners (2026-08-11)
A pass over the modules no previous audit had reached, chosen by cross-referencing every app module against its test-reference count and its mentions in this file. Three real defects, and the reusable result is again the playbook's thesis: **every one was a class this file already documents, surviving in the module the original fix did not reach.**

### ONE unreadable read at LAUNCH destroyed the whole sync library
The "unreadable is not empty" sweep hardened `core/library.py`, `core/preset_manager.py`, `ui/auth.py`, `core.today_store` and `atomic_update_sync_pairs`. **`core/library_migrate.py` was never swept** - and it is the worst possible member of that family to miss: it sits UPSTREAM of the hardened store, so it destroys the library before `load_library`'s guards can run, and it is the only one that runs on **every launch**.
- **The legacy stores are never deleted** (the migration only copies them to `*.bak`), so a re-run always has something to import - and importing REBUILDS the library from its state at first migration. `needs_migration` answered "yes, migrate" for any library file it could not read, and `_read_json` swallowed `OSError` alongside `JSONDecodeError`. **Reproduced against the real functions**: one transient `OSError` - an antivirus lock, a config dir on an offline share, a permissions blip - and a pair the user had renamed reverted to its original name while a second saved pair **disappeared entirely**. Every saved pair, name, group and daily-sync membership since the first migration, gone at launch, with a debug-level log as the only trace.
- **Three outcomes now, not two**: `None` (absent), `_DAMAGED` (read it, cannot use it), `_READ_FAILED` (could not read it). Transient -> **refuse to migrate, touch nothing** (one launch of Canvas names costs nothing; proceeding is permanent). Damaged -> **quarantine the bytes first, then rebuild** - which is the docstring's original reasoning, and it was only ever wrong for a blip. Same asymmetry, same verdict, as the sqlite corruption whitelist: destroy nothing on the strength of an error you have not identified.
- **A LEGACY file that exists and cannot be read aborts the migration**, writing nothing. Treating it as empty would build a library missing every pair it held, and `needs_migration` then answers False **for ever** - permanent and silent. Aborting costs one launch; the next retries.
- `UnicodeDecodeError` (a SIBLING of `JSONDecodeError`, both `ValueError`) escaped `_read_json` AND `needs_migration`, which `migrate_if_needed` calls **outside its own try** - so its "never raises" docstring was false. `app.py`'s blanket handler saved the launch, which is exactly why nobody noticed.
- Covered by `tests/test_library_migration_hardening.py` (13). **Its `_unreadable` is a context manager, not `monkeypatch` + `undo()`, and that is not style**: `undo()` reverts the fixture's `CANVAS_DL_CONFIG_DIR` too, so assertions after it read **the developer's real library in the repo root**. The tests caught it themselves by failing with an empty pair list; `_assert_isolated` now refuses to assert outside the tmp dir.

### The last gate before a sync could not warn about a FULL disk
`format_available_space` fixed what the Confirm Sync dialog PRINTS. The arithmetic beside it still could not tell three cases apart, and two were wrong.
- The ratio was gated on `avail_bytes > 0`, so a genuinely **full** volume fell to the same 1% floor as an empty one and the ">70% of remaining space" notice could not fire. Measured on the dialog's own expression: **0.4 MB free warned, 0 B free did not.** The one case the warning exists for was the only one it could not reach.
- An **unreadable** volume drew a 1% bar - while the code's own comment claimed the maths "suppresses the bar instead of drawing a false one". It did not: the floor applies whenever `total_bytes > 0`.
- `shared/helpers.disk_fill_percent` has three outcomes: **`None`** (never measured - draw nothing, warn nothing), **`100.0`** (measured, no room), else the linear ratio with the 1% floor. Extracted rather than left inline for the reason `format_available_space`'s own history records: a test that re-implements the dialog's expression tests the copy, and four mutants survived on exactly that.
- `ui/sync_review.py` blocks a full volume before the dialog is reached (it demands 1 GB), so the full-disk half is belt-and-braces; the unmeasured half is not. Verified in the browser in all four states - full 100% + amber, tight 83% + amber, plenty 1%, unknown an empty track reading as "not measured".

### TWO live progress-bar renderers, and only one was hardened
`_pct` exists because the value goes into `width: N%`, where `-3` and `nan` are **invalid CSS**: the browser drops the declaration and a block div falls back to the full track, so "less than nothing happened" renders as "finished". That hardening reached `build_progress_bar_html` and **not `shared/helpers.render_progress_bar`**, the other live renderer - called three times from `sync/execution.py`'s run loop. Measured before the fix: **NaN raised `ValueError`, inf raised `OverflowError`, None raised `TypeError`**, from inside a repaint path whose values come from counters owned by several subsystems. Now routed through the same `_pct`/`_num`: one clamp, imported rather than copied (function-scoped, because `progress_dashboard` imports `shared.theme`).
- The two renderers still have different signatures and different visuals (`engine/progress_dashboard` draws the dashboard bar; `shared/helpers` draws the sync-execution bar with a centred label). That is an observation, not a fixed defect - unifying them is a visual change to the sync run screen, which needs its own before/after pass.

### Sweeps that came back CLEAN - do not repeat them
Each was mechanical and whole-project, so a future hit is genuinely new code:
- **0** text-mode `open()` without an explicit `encoding` (the CP1252 rule is fully honoured).
- **0** JSON reads guarded without `UnicodeDecodeError`/`ValueError` - that sibling class is fully swept.
- Every truncate-in-place write is a log, a temp file or a write-probe; every user store goes through tmp + `os.replace`.
- Every `rmtree` target is an app-owned config path. `model_dir(model_id)` cannot take user input - `MODEL_REGISTRY` is hard-coded in the source.
- Both untimed `subprocess.run` calls are the macOS folder pickers, which must NOT time out (an arbitrarily long human decision); every other external process carries a timeout, and `panopto/hardware.py` probes at 4 s.
- `core/canvas_debug.py` is bounded at 5 MB with tail retention and a truncation marker; `core/cancellation.py` audits clean (events checked before session state, terminal states excluded so the user is never trapped).
- Of **17** function names defined in more than one module, all but `render_progress_bar` are thin aliases over a single shared implementation (`make_long_path`, `_cleanup_sync_state`, `_add_pair_lazy`, and the `panopto.settings`/`shared.legal` config trio, which both delegate to `shared.helpers.read_json_for_update`).
- Every numeric division on a counter is guarded. The one exception was cosmetic and is now closed: `converters/archive.py`'s bomb MESSAGE interpolated `uncompressed / archive_size` unguarded while the condition beside it guarded the same division, and the condition's other clause is true independently of `archive_size` - so the line meant to explain a declined archive could raise `ZeroDivisionError` instead. Unreachable today (a 0-byte file cannot parse as an archive), fixed anyway because the reachability argument depends on a fact two libraries away.

### Known, deliberately NOT changed
- **Deleting a Whisper model is one unconfirmed click.** `ui/panopto_page.py`'s trash icon calls `delete_model` immediately, while the CUDA libraries on the same page use a carefully-built schedule + undo flow with a comment explaining why a same-shaped second button reads as a confirm. The inconsistency is real and the cost is bandwidth and time, not data. It is left for a pass that can verify it properly: a confirm state changes the row's element SHAPE, which is the container-inheritance hazard, and that needs its own before/after browser pass rather than a hurried one.
- **The cancel Events in `core/cancellation.py` are process-global**, so two browser sessions against one server would share them. That is the correct trade, not an oversight: a background thread has no Streamlit session context, which is the whole reason the Events exist.

### Never run the mutation harness on a DIRTY source tree
This file already warns that a concurrent commit can CAPTURE a mutant. The same hazard runs the other way: the harness's `restore()` is a hard `git checkout`, so **any source change not yet committed is discarded silently**, and the results then describe a tree nobody wrote. It ate two finished fixes on 2026-08-11 (`file_in_scope`'s unquote and the duplicate-panel removal); the tests written for them are what noticed, by failing. Commit first, every time - the harness now refuses to start on a dirty tree.

### A dialog's height belongs on its CONTENT region, never on the dialog (2026-08-11)
**`div[role="dialog"] > div:first-child` is NOT the padded body.** Measured in Chrome: the dialog has **three** children - `child[0]` a chrome wrapper (24/12 padding), `child[1]` the content body (12/24), `child[2]` the close button. A `min-height: 300px` on the first-child selector therefore inflated the WRAPPER, pushing the title from `top=62` to `top=290` and leaving a 300px empty band **above** it. The older note in this file about padding living on `> div:first-child` does not license putting a HEIGHT there.
- **The fix is to keep the region that normally holds content.** The Ignored Files dialog already had a sized `st.container(height=filelist_height, key=f"{prefix}_filelist")`; it was simply skipped when the list was empty, which is what let the dialog collapse. It now always renders, with the empty-state notice INSIDE it. Verified: an empty dialog is **777px, identical to a populated one**, content area 480px, notice inside it. The hub does the same via one `HUB_LIST_HEIGHT` constant read by both its populated card list and its empty-state container, so the two cannot drift.
- **The Smart Select collapsed header: the dead band IS the card's own `padding-bottom`, and removing it is the WHOLE fix.** Also pulling the label up by `-card_pad_y` double-counts, and the label keeps its own `padding-bottom` - measured **card 39px against label 49px**, i.e. the hover strip (which is the clickable area) hung 11px BELOW the card containing it. With only `padding-bottom: 0` the card is 51px and the label 49px, the 2px being the card's own borders: flush, no dead band, no overhang.
- **THE PROCESS RULE, from the operator and now non-negotiable: a UI change is not done until it has been looked at in a browser, BEFORE and AFTER.** Both defects above are geometry, invisible in code review and obvious in one screenshot. Two mechanics that make it cheap when the real screen needs a Canvas token: drive the REAL dialog function with a stubbed `sync_manager` (the CSS, keys and markup are the app's - only the data is fake), and remember the harness runs with `--server.fileWatcherType none`, so **restart it after every edit** or you will measure the previous version and conclude your fix did nothing.
- **Confirming a folder picker without navigating must look like a no-op.** `_folder_pick_callback` ran `_auto_detect_course_from_manifest` on every pick, so Edit -> Change Folder -> Choose announced a course auto-detected from a folder the user had not changed. Now gated on `norm_folder_key(new) != norm_folder_key(prev)` - the one folder normaliser, so a trailing separator is not a change; in ADD mode `prev` is empty, so the first pick still auto-detects, which is the case worth having.

### A folder picker must open where you can CHOOSE, not inside what you already have
`choose folder default location <the pair's own folder>` opens the panel INSIDE that folder, so "Change Folder" landed in a directory whose contents are all irrelevant (a course folder's own PDFs) and the user had to navigate **up** before picking anything - and re-pointing a pair at a moved or renamed sibling is the common case. Reported on macOS and Windows. `shared/helpers.picker_start_for_existing()` returns the PARENT, is applied at the four re-choose sites (sync add/edit, hub add/edit-pair, rescue mode), and is written **once** so the two platforms' pickers cannot disagree - macOS's `choose folder` and Windows' IFileOpenDialog both take their directory from it. Destination pickers (download path) are deliberately untouched: opening inside the last-used destination is right there. Non-existent, empty and root paths pass through unchanged so each caller's own fallback chain (session default -> ~/Downloads) still applies.

### A JS-gated button must never render UNGATED - the gate outlives the dialog (2026-08-11)
`shared/components.live_enable_button` greys a genuinely-enabled Save button while its name field is invalid. It does that by injecting CSS into the **PARENT document**, keyed on the button key (`cd-live-css-<key>`) behind `if (!doc.getElementById(STYLE_ID))` - so the style is written **once and never removed for the life of the session** - and its polarity is `button:not([data-cd-valid="1"])`, i.e. a MISSING marker means disabled, with only the bridge ever setting that marker.
- **So any render that shows the button WITHOUT calling the helper inherits a permanently greyed, `pointer-events: none` button.** Reported as *"I wrote the pair name but couldn't save"*, with the disabled cursor on hover. The Save Group and Save Pair dialogs are ONE function sharing ONE button key (`save_group_create`), and the pair path deliberately skips gating when the course supplies a suggested name (its Save must be live from frame 1, because empty means "use the course's own name"). Open Save as Group once - which always gates - and every later Save as Pair was dead. **Deleting the saved group does not help**: the style is already in the document, and nothing removes it.
- **Unique keys per dialog would NOT have been enough.** The same trap exists inside the pair dialog alone: a course whose name yields no suggestion gates and injects the style, and the next course that does supply one then renders ungated into a document that still greys it.
- **The fix is `active=`, and the call must be UNCONDITIONAL.** `live_enable_button(..., active=<condition>)` tears the previous gate down when it does not apply (removes the `<style>`, clears `data-cd-valid` and the reason tooltip) instead of leaving it standing. A conditional CALL is the bug; a conditional VALUE is fine.
- **Do NOT "fix" this by flipping the polarity to `[data-cd-valid="0"]`.** Fail-closed while the bridge is running is correct - an unmarked button that looks clickable lets a click land on an empty name and silently do nothing. A mutation asserting the polarity is caught.
- **The safe pattern already existed one screen over**: the hub's rename row renders the button and calls the gate inside the SAME `if`, so key and gate are always co-present. `tests/test_ui_leak_and_pair_scoping.py` encodes exactly that invariant - a conditional gate is only allowed when the `st.button(key=...)` it names renders inside the same branch - so the next gated button cannot repeat this without failing.

### A per-FOLDER fact keyed on the COURSE, and markup that dedent could not flatten (2026-08-11)
Both found by the operator driving the real app, and both are classes this file already states elsewhere - reached in modules the earlier fixes did not.
- **"Ignored Files (N)" was cached by `course_id` alone.** Ignored files live in ONE folder's `.canvas_sync.db`, so the identity is the LINK `(course_id, folder)`. With the same course synced into two folders - an ordinary thing, e.g. re-downloading somewhere tidier - the build loop OVERWROTE the entry and both cards read the last pair's data. Measured against the real manifests: folder A held **0** ignored rows, folder B **23**, and both cards said 23. **The dangerous half is not the number**: the cached entry carries the `sync_manager`, so opening the dialog from the wrong card handed it the OTHER folder's manifest and a restore there would have written to a folder the user was not looking at. The dialog's widget keys were `cign_{course_id}` too, so two pairs of one course shared every checkbox. Now keyed through **`core.pair_labels.pair_key`**, which already existed as this app's one answer to "which link is this?" - a second local key here is exactly how two consumers of one identity drift apart. The same raw-tuple comparison in `_saved_pair_sigs` was normalised with it (a JSON-roundtripped `"43660"` never matched an int `43660`, so an already-saved pair read as unsaved and could be saved twice).
- **`{pan_note}` alone on a line printed the config viewer's own `<div>` as text.** The blank-line trap this file documents for the hub pair card, in `render_config_summary_badges`. It fires only when the optional part is ABSENT - i.e. when Panopto is switched ON, the normal case.
  - **The reason the identical shape is harmless a dozen other places is `st.markdown`'s own transform**: `streamlit.string_util.clean_text` is `textwrap.dedent(...).strip()`. An f-string whose lines are UNIFORMLY indented is flattened to column 0, so its blank line is followed by an unindented line and nothing happens - that is the completion card. The Panopto case differed in one respect: the assembled grid also contains column-0 lines, so the common prefix is 0, **dedent removes nothing**, and the 4-space line survives as an indented CODE BLOCK.
  - **Fixing the one guilty interpolation is NOT enough, and the attempt proved it.** Rebuilding `pan_html` as a single line MOVED the fault: `grid_container` interpolates it at 4-space indent, so the blank line then preceded a 4-space line for the first time, in BOTH Panopto branches. The function now normalises `\s*\n\s*` to a single space at its one return, which removes the class rather than an instance. A space, not `""` - a newline between tags is already whitespace to the browser, so the rendering is unchanged.
  - **`scripts/verify_architecture.py` Rule 10 gates it** (0 violations): an interpolation alone on a line of element HTML, whose name is provably assignable to `""`, where the f-string also has a column-0 line so dedent is a no-op. All four conditions are load-bearing; without the dedent test it produces two false positives (the completion card and the sync-review header), and **`<style>`/`<script>`/`<pre>` are exempt because they are CommonMark type-1 blocks that end at their closing tag and are NOT terminated by a blank line** - three sites rely on that.
- **Three UI-truth fixes in the same pass.** The Settings FDA card rendered **no button once access was granted**, making the one screen that explains the permission a dead end - it now offers a monochrome "Manage access" in that state, which also makes the keyed container emit the same child count in both branches (it was 2 vs 1, and Streamlit hands a block the children of whatever occupied its index). The sync **analysis** phase was the only phase of its flow with no `step-header` heading while `Syncing...`, `Sync Complete!` and `Panopto Recordings` all have one. And the Ignored Files dialog had no empty states: Smart Select opened onto a bare divider once nothing was left to select, and restoring the last file left the dialog blank because the success notice is **popped as it renders** - so the empty state has to be an `elif` on it, or the next rerun shows nothing.
- Covered by `tests/test_ui_leak_and_pair_scoping.py` (22).

### The Accessibility permission was not EARNED - removed 2026-08-10, do not add it back
The app used to prepend `set visible of (first process whose name is "Microsoft Word") to false` (System Events) to **every** Office conversion, to stop Word's start-screen gallery flashing past. That one line raised **"Canvas Downloader would like to control this computer using accessibility features"** - and it is gone. `engine/applescript_bridge` now runs no AppleScript that touches another process at all.
- **Why it mattered more than a cosmetic tweak**: it is the worst prompt macOS has for a first run. It has **no Allow button** (only *Open System Settings* and a visually primary *Deny*), it cannot be granted from the dialog at all, and it says "control this computer" - on an app that ships **unsigned**, where the user's trust is already thin. Onboarding friction is the thing that kills adoption here, so a permission has to pay for itself.
- **It also hid the USER'S OWN Office session.** Hiding a *process* hides all of its windows, not only ours. Measured: a Word window the user had opened themselves, their own document on screen, went `visible=true -> false` the instant a conversion started. That is a worse defect than the flicker it was suppressing.
- **And it bought NOTHING.** Measured on the real converter, cold Word, two conversions back to back, sampling `visible of process` every 0.25 s - the only sound way to compare, since an in-vitro AppleScript run hits the powerbox trap instead (see below):

  | mechanism | Word visible |
  |---|---|
  | System Events hide (the old code) | 2/11 samples |
  | `open -g -j -a` instead | 1/12 - 2/11 |
  | **neither** | **0/7, repeatable** |

  **Doing nothing is the quietest of the three.** An Apple event to a not-running app (`tell application "Microsoft Word" to open ...`) launches it *without activating it*, so it comes up already hidden - the trace goes straight `absent -> false`. An explicit `open -g -j -a` is what introduces a brief visible blip, during its own launch. Verified identically for **Excel and PowerPoint** (`absent -> false`, never visible, frontmost stayed Finder): all three converters share ONE runner (`run_applescript`), which is why one deletion covered three apps - the same counting rule as the Office delete-gates.
- **`prime_office_automation` still launches with `open -g -j` and that is right**: its job is to batch the one-time Automation prompts up front, so it must launch the apps, and `-j` keeps that launch hidden. Its blip happens once per run, while the user is being shown a permission notice anyway.
- **What is UNAVOIDABLE is the dock ICON** appearing while Office runs - the app genuinely is running. It does not bounce, because nothing activates it.
- **Every prompt the app now raises can be answered in place**, which is what makes `TCC_FIRST_RUN_NOTICE`'s "Click Allow or OK on each" true for the first time: Automation (per Office app + System Events) → Allow; the macOS 15 "access data from other apps" powerbox → OK. That constant lives beside the mechanism precisely so the two cannot drift; a change that reintroduces an Accessibility prompt makes its copy a lie.
- **The prompt was introduced in v2.0.1 and shipped in no audited build** - `v2.0.0`'s bridge contains no `set visible of`, which is exactly why the operator's earlier macOS audits never saw the dialog and remembered conversions as clean. A "we always had this" instinct would have been wrong; check the tag.
- **THE MEASUREMENT TRAP, and it cost a full cycle**: driving the conversion AppleScript **directly** on a path outside the Office container makes macOS demand the per-folder *"Grant File Access"* powerbox prompt, which blocks and returns `AppleEvent timed out (-1712)` with no PDF - and reads exactly like the hide having broken the conversion. This is the same trap the staging note records from the other direction. **Always drive the REAL converter** (`converters.word.WordToPDF().convert`), which stages into the container first. Also: a hand-built minimal `.docx` makes Word raise a repair modal and hang; `textutil -convert doc` produces a genuine legacy `.doc` that Word opens silently.
- Covered by `tests/test_macos_no_accessibility_permission.py`; all 7 mutations of the real code are caught. **Its scanner strips COMMENTS but deliberately keeps STRING LITERALS** - AppleScript reaches osascript as a Python string, so a real regression lives inside a literal, and an earlier version that blanked literals let a re-added System Events hide survive. Synthetic-input words (`keystroke`, `click at`) are only scanned in files containing `osascript`: this app's JavaScript bridges say "one rerun per keystroke" in their comments, which produced four false positives.

### `normcase` was deciding an UNLINK, and it is the identity off Windows (2026-08-10)
The `_path_key` fix earlier the same day taught that `os.path.normcase` does nothing on macOS while the default volume is case-INSENSITIVE. The sweep for that class elsewhere found the same primitive deciding a **deletion**, and this one destroys the user's file:
- **`sync/execution.py`'s superseded-copy guard.** After a clean update of a RENAMED secondary entity, the regenerate writes the canonical name and the old pristine copy is removed - guarded by `normcase(old) != normcase(new)` so it can "never delete the file it just wrote". On macOS a **case-only** Canvas rename (`week 1 assignment` -> `Week 1 Assignment`, an ordinary lecturer edit) makes those two strings differ while naming ONE file. Reproduced: file written, guard says "different", `unlink()` - **the folder is left empty and the manifest row points at nothing.** Now keyed through `_path_key`.
- **`_claim_target`** reserved dispatch paths by `normcase`, so two Canvas files whose sanitized names differ only in case each claimed a "free" path and then wrote over each other.
- **`protect_conversion_target` / `is_conversion_target_protected`** keyed the `_NewVersion` protection set by `normcase(resolve())`. **`Path.resolve()` does NOT canonicalise case on macOS**, so a mark written as `Notes.md` and a lookup for `notes.md` miss - and a missed mark overwrites the student's edited conversion output, which is the single thing that set exists to prevent.
- **`_path_key('')` is `'.'`**, because `normpath('')` is - so an emptiness test must be made on the RAW value. Keying first turned "no recorded path" into a real lookup for the current directory; caught by a mutation, not by reading.

### Building the macOS bundle: two traps
- **`excludes` cannot beat `collect_all`.** Adding a package to `scripts/build_excludes.py` stops it being IMPORTED; `collect_all` also runs `collect_data_files`, which copies the package in as **DATA** regardless. `pync` survived an exclude for exactly that reason and had to be removed from the spec's `collect_all` list *and* its explicit `binaries` entry.
- **`pync` broke the whole signature.** It vendors a nested `terminal-notifier.app`, PyInstaller rewrites `.` to `__dot__` in those directory names, and `codesign --verify --strict` then fails for the ENTIRE app ("the main executable or Info.plist must be a regular file"). Harmless while the app ships ad-hoc/unsigned, so this is a **latent** blocker, not a current one - but notarization rejects it, and `--force --deep` silently produces an unverifiable artifact. Dropped: it is notification fallback #3 of 4, its import is already guarded, and the primary `UNUserNotificationCenter` path is verified working. After the change `--verify --strict` and `--verify --deep --strict` are both rc=0 with the `apple-events` entitlement intact.
- **Verify in the BUNDLE, not just in source - and know which half you verified.** Pointing a browser at the packaged app's Streamlit port exercises the frozen backend (bundled modules, real config dir, real keychain, signed binary) and **not** WKWebView rendering; that needs a screenshot of the app's own window. Both halves were covered separately. The packaged app was proven to: verify strictly, convert Word→PDF (genuine `%PDF`, source deleted only after verification, manifest repointed), honour the short keychain probe, record exactly one clean exit, and render the FDA nudge.

## Platform Notes
- **Windows**: `win32com.client` COM automation for Office → PDF conversions. `ctypes` to hide `.canvas_sync.db`.
- **macOS**: `osascript` (AppleScript) via `engine/applescript_bridge.py` for Office conversions. Requires `com.apple.security.automation.apple-events` entitlement in `.spec`.
- **Keyring**: Lazy-imported inside functions on all platforms (`ui/auth.py`). macOS = Keychain with a 90s watchdog: a CLEAN install never prompts (creating/reading your own item is silent); only a REBUILD with a new ad-hoc signature reading the previous build's item triggers the one-time "enter login keychain password" prompt. Windows = Credential Manager (5s watchdog) + DPAPI-encrypted `.token_fallback` file; macOS deliberately has NO disk fallback.
- **File I/O**: Always specify `encoding='utf-8'` explicitly - Windows defaults to CP1252, causing Mojibake in emoji-heavy UI files.

## Build
- Windows: `pyinstaller Canvas_Downloader.spec`
- macOS: `pyinstaller --clean Canvas_Downloader_macOS.spec` + `codesign --force --deep -s - --entitlements entitlements.mac.plist Canvas\ Downloader.app`
- Launcher: `start.py` - daemonized Streamlit thread + `pywebview.start()` on main thread (required for macOS Cocoa).

### Bundle size: the policy lives in `scripts/build_excludes.py`, not in the specs
Measured 2026-07-27 on Windows: **462.8 MB → 326.2 MB installed** (-136.6 MB, -29.5%), **installer 133.7 MB → 87.3 MB** (-34.7%), 5,865 → 4,840 files, and the PYZ from **5,322 → 2,324 modules**.

- **Both specs load ONE shared policy module** (`scripts/build_excludes.py`) the same way they already load `patch_streamlit_webkit`. It is one file on purpose: the specs are near-duplicates, and a trimming fix applied to only one of them is invisible in review and ships a fat build on the other OS. **Do not add a one-off `excludes=[...]` entry to a spec.**
- **Root cause of the bloat: `collect_all()`.** It is `collect_submodules` + `collect_data_files` + `collect_dynamic_libs`, and `collect_submodules` walks **every** submodule - test suites, CLI entry points, optional integrations - forcing each in as a hidden import; PyInstaller then follows *their* imports. Three chains, read off the build's own `xref-*.html`: `huggingface_hub._oauth`→fastapi→pydantic, `huggingface_hub.inference._mcp`→mcp→pydantic_settings→{azure.core→opentelemetry→**grpc**, google.auth→**cryptography**}; `psutil.tests`→**pip**→`cachetools.redis_cache`→**redis** and `numpy.typing.tests`→**_pytest**; `webview.http`→bottle→**gevent**(274 modules)→dnspython.
- **Three mechanisms, and the order matters.** `lean_filter` (passed as `filter_submodules=`) is the root-cause fix and is **safe by construction** - it only stops a module being *force-added*, so anything a real import reaches is still collected normally. `excludes_for()` is the blunt instrument that severs chains reached through genuine imports (bottle→gevent), so every entry there is justified in the module. `strip_test_datas(a.datas)` runs **after** `Analysis` because `collect_data_files` copies test `.py` files back in as *data* even once they are out of the import graph (7.29 MB, 6.36 MB of it numpy's); it touches data only and can never make an import fail.
- **THE TRAP - never exclude the bare `google` namespace.** The huggingface chain reaches `google.auth`, which makes `google` look dead. **Streamlit needs `google.protobuf` for every `ForwardMsg` it sends** - excluding it bricks the app. Only `google.auth`/`google.oauth2` are dead. This was caught by the validation harness, not by reading code.
- **Validate by BLOCKING, not by reasoning.** Install a `sys.meta_path` finder that raises `ImportError` for every candidate, then run the real paths (Streamlit + `ForwardMsg_pb2`, all app modules, canvasapi, `BeautifulSoup(..., "html.parser")` + `.select()`, `webview.http`, keyring, win11toast, win32com, moviepy+PIL, `snapshot_download`, a real transcription). Minutes instead of a 10-minute build cycle, and it names *which* exclusion broke *which* path. **Run transcription in a clean process** - co-loading streamlit/numpy/win32com with ctranslate2 segfaults from an OpenMP clash *with or without* any exclusions, which is exactly why `panopto/transcribe_worker.py` exists; a combined harness will crash and look like your change did it.

### The bundle shipped FFmpeg TWICE - `scripts/patch_faster_whisper_audio.py`
`ffmpeg.exe` (83.6 MB, used by `converters/video.py` and `panopto/stream.py`) **and** PyAV's `av.libs/` (62.0 MB of libav DLLs - avcodec 18.3, libx265 12.0, libSvtAv1Enc 7.3). PyAV was pulled in by **one line** - `faster_whisper/audio.py`'s `import av` - to decode an MP3 this app produced itself, moments earlier, with the other FFmpeg. The x265 and SVT-AV1 *video encoders* have nothing to do with transcribing a lecture.
- The patch rewrites `decode_audio()` to drive the bundled binary (same pattern as `patch_streamlit_webkit.py`), then `av` is excluded: **-65.4 MB**.
- **Verified equivalent, not assumed:** decoded the same MP3 both ways - for a `str` path (the only form this app passes) max abs sample diff **0.00000000**, Pearson r **1.0**, identical length. A file-like object keeps ~1.9 ms of extra tail because trimming an MP3's encoder delay needs a *seekable* input and `pipe:0` is not; nothing here uses that form.
- It is **version-gated** (`_TESTED_VERSIONS`) and raises at build time on an untested faster-whisper rather than silently shipping a wrong decoder. When you bump the pin in `requirements.txt`, run one real transcription and add the version.
- Because it edits site-packages in place, `streamlit run app.py` uses the patched decoder too - dev and frozen behave identically. `pip install --force-reinstall faster-whisper` reverts it; re-run the script.

### Deliberately NOT done - decided 2026-07-27, do not re-litigate
- **`moviepy` stays** (~6.5 MB with PIL + imageio). `converters/video.py` uses `VideoFileClip` for one thing - extracting audio to MP3 - which is a single ffmpeg call the codebase already knows how to make, and dropping it would also delete `_safe_close`'s ThreadPoolExecutor + psutil-kill machinery (which exists *only* because moviepy's `close()` blocks on `Popen.communicate()`). **Declined anyway:** the ffmpeg/Panopto path cost a lot of debugging to get working and ~6.5 MB does not justify disturbing it. Note PIL cannot be dropped separately - `moviepy/video/VideoClip.py` imports it at module level.
- **`onnxruntime` stays** (37.3 MB). It is only the Silero VAD backend and `panopto/transcribe.py` already retries with `vad_filter=False` (`_is_vad_engine_error` matches the `"onnxruntime"` in the raised message, so the fallback genuinely fires). Kept because VAD skips silence, which on lecture recordings is both faster *and* what stops Whisper hallucinating over long quiet stretches.
- **`tkinter` stays on Windows** (7.1 MB) - it is `shared/helpers.py:pick_folder`. Excluded on **macOS**, where it is pure dead weight: that branch returns from `osascript` on every path (`helpers.py:501`), so the tkinter import below it is unreachable.
- **UPX: no.** The Inno installer already uses `lzma2/ultra64` + `SolidCompression`, and UPX-then-LZMA compresses *worse* than LZMA alone - it would not shrink the download at all, only slow startup and attract antivirus heuristics on an installer with no EV signature.
- **Downgrading imageio-ffmpeg: no.** 0.5.1 ships a 61.7 MB binary (-22 MB) but it is FFmpeg **4.2.2** (2019); Panopto HLS delivery, modern AAC/HEVC and current TLS are not worth regressing.
- **Still unmeasured: the macOS PyObjC surface.** All the platform-agnostic wins above apply to the mac build, but `collect_all('UserNotifications')` may pull far more pyobjc than needed. That needs a mac to measure - do not guess at it blind.

## The login institution picker: a shortcut that must never become a gate (2026-08-07)
A searchable directory of Canvas institutions sits to the RIGHT of the "Your Canvas URL" field; picking one writes its address into that field. The field stays fully editable, so the picker is a **shortcut and never a gate** - every one of the thousands of Canvas schools NOT in the list works exactly as before. That framing is load-bearing in the copy: an always-visible caveat under the row, plus an empty state worded as an instruction ("Your school still works"), because a student whose school is missing must not conclude the app does not support them.
- **The most dangerous failure is not a missing school, it is the WRONG one.** Omitting a school costs one extra paste into a field that is right there; a wrong entry hands the student a real, working Canvas login page belonging to a different university, with nothing on screen suggesting the address was the problem. Every gate below is sized by that asymmetry.
- **`scripts/build_institution_list.py` is the generator and the app never talks to Instructure's finder.** The pipeline is crawl (~7,200 published accounts - the hard ceiling; many large universities simply are not discoverable) -> match seeds for clean display names -> fill from the crawl under each account's OWN name -> verify -> hand-review reject list. **The FILL half carries no pairing risk at all**, because nothing is mapped to anything: the account's own name beside its own domain. That is why coverage can be raised without re-auditing pairings, and it is the property the whole widening rests on.
- **THE APP IS NOT UNIVERSITY-ONLY, and the generator used to assume it was (corrected 2026-08-09).** Every institution and every level that uses Canvas is in the target group - erhvervsakademier, VUC, gymnasier, konservatorier, K-12 districts. The old fill gate `_HIGHER_ED` allowed only English/Spanish/Portuguese/German HIGHER-ED words, so it excluded two whole categories at once: every non-university institution, and every institution of any level whose own published name is Nordic, Finnish, Dutch, French or Italian. **Denmark shipped two entries** while Erhvervsakademi Aarhus, Den Danske Filmskole, VUC Roskilde, Det Kgl. Danske Musikkonservatorium and IBA all verified as live Canvas hosts. The gate is now `is_institution()`: an accent-FOLDED multilingual allowlist (`_EDUCATION`), minus things that are not schools or are a school's side entrance (`_NOT_AN_INSTITUTION` - churches, vendors, hospitals, alumni/catalog/continuing-ed/parent tenants), plus `_ACADEMIC_TLD` as standalone proof (`.edu`, `.ac.*`, `.edu.*`, `.sch.*`) for accounts published only as an acronym. **1,797 -> 4,272 rows, and 0 of the old candidates lost - it is a strict superset.**
  - **Only the FILL gate moved. `_BAD_ACCOUNT`, `accepts()`, `contradicts()` and `corroborates()` are untouched**, so no seed pairing can change because of a coverage decision. `tests/test_institution_gate.py` builds the list twice with the fill gate forced wide open and asserts every seed row is identical - that separation is what makes raising coverage safe, and it must not be wired together again.
  - Declined on measurement, do not re-litigate: **domain-side** education-word matching (admits 18 accounts, nine of them Yamaha music schools) and a **blocklist-only** fill gate (+1,939 rows at ~35% junk - Hubspot, franklincovey, a medical-software vendor). The allowlist is what keeps the list trustworthy at 4,272 rows.
- **The SEED ranking needs `domain_rank` as its last key, or a tie is decided by crawl order.** `jaccard` scores token SETS, so "University of Michigan - Ann Arbor", "- Dearborn" and "- Flint" are all exactly 1.00 against the seed, as are "University of Kansas - KU" and "- kuconnect.ku.edu"; corroboration and ccTLD tie with them, and `key > best[0]` then keeps whichever the crawl dict yielded FIRST. Measured 2026-08-09: Michigan resolved to **Dearborn** and Kansas to `kuconnect`. The fix belongs in the seed key, not in the dedupe - that pass already sorts by `domain_rank`, but core rows are ordered ahead of fill rows, so by then the seed's choice is frozen. A tie test that does not feed the pool in the LOSING order passes with the fix removed; `tests/test_institution_gate.py` runs both orders for exactly that reason.
- **`LOCAL_NAMES` must be looked up ACCENT-FOLDED - the two tables disagree about spelling.** `SEEDS` writes "Malmo University", "Umea University", "Linkoping University"; `LOCAL_NAMES` keys the same schools with their accents. A plain `dict.get` therefore returned `None` and those universities shipped with no Swedish name at all - not in the label, and not as a matcher probe, which also means a locally-named account and its English seed read as two different schools. It fails silently: `display_name` just returns the English name, which looks deliberate. `local_name()` folds the key; **8 further `LOCAL_NAMES` entries had no seed at all** and are now seeded, because LOCAL_NAMES is only ever consulted FOR a seed.
- **The shipped data can be AHEAD of the generator.** The pre-2026-08-09 module contained "Högskolan Dalarna (Dalarna University)" and "Technische Universität Nürnberg (...)", which the code at that commit could not reproduce - so a regeneration silently dropped them. After any regeneration, diff against the previous module and explain every REMOVED row before shipping; that diff is what found three of the four defects in this section.
- **Verification is unreliable at scale, and one retry pass is not enough.** A 16-way sweep over ~4,500 domains reported ~180 "genuinely unreachable" hosts, of which 16 sampled by hand answered on the FIRST try individually, every one in under 1.5 s - including the University of Kansas, Georgetown, Rhode Island School of Design and Wake Technical Community College. Retrying the same way reproduces it; `verify_many` now runs gradually gentler passes (16 workers -> 3 -> 1) with a cooldown. **That makes a full rebuild take ~2.7 hours**, almost all of it in the final serial pass over hosts that really are dead. To iterate, feed the previous run's verdicts back as a cache (every shipped domain verified `ok`) and verify only what is new - nothing in the generator changes what a host answers, and it turns a rebuild into ~6 minutes.
- **The three hand-review gates do different jobs and `dedupe_key` can undo one of them.** `REJECT` blocks a seed->domain pairing and DROPS that seed entirely (it does not fall through to the runner-up); `REJECT_DOMAINS` drops a host outright; `RENAME` relabels a real tenant whose own name over-claims. **A RENAME label must not use a parenthetical or a " - " tail**: `dedupe_key` runs `clean_name` again on the finished label, strips the qualifier straight back off, and the row collides with the one it was renamed to be distinct FROM. Measured: `hkust-gz` renamed to "... (Guangzhou)" vanished entirely instead of sitting beside the main campus.
- **A SIMILARITY SCORE CANNOT ANSWER THIS QUESTION - ten wrong universities shipped before that was understood (2026-08-08).** Symmetric scoring above was necessary and not sufficient. "University of British Columbia" vs "Columbia University" scores 0.667, the domain `courseworks2.columbia.edu` genuinely corroborates the shared token, and every gate agreed - because the one word that decides it, **`british`**, is exactly what a score discards. Same shape put **Duke Kunshan on Duke's Canvas, Colorado State on CU Boulder's, Oklahoma State on Oklahoma Christian's, Central Florida on Central State's, North Texas on North Park's, South Carolina on South Alabama's, Manchester Met on the University of Manchester's, UWA on Western Colorado's and American University in Dubai on American University DC's**. All ten were live; a student picking their own school got a real, working login page for a different university with nothing on screen suggesting the address was wrong.
  - **`contradicts()` asks the question a score cannot**: does either name carry a *distinctive* token (non-generic, so not "university"/"state"/"college"/"royal") that the other lacks **and the domain does not vouch for**? A one-sided uncorroborated word is not a spelling variation, it is another school. It must veto **one-sided** - requiring both sides to differ lets `British Columbia -> Columbia University` straight back through.
  - **THE SECOND DEFECT, and the reason rejection alone would have made things worse:** `corroborates()` built the acronym from EVERY word, so "University of Central Florida" produced `uocf` and never `ucf`. **A school whose Canvas host is its own acronym could not corroborate its own domain** (`ucf`, `unt`, `ubc` all failed), so the matcher passed over the RIGHT host and settled for a wrong one that happened to corroborate. Fixing the veto without this would have deleted five real universities instead of repointing them. Corroboration also has to work in **both directions** (`colostate` -> `colo` is a *contraction*, a prefix of the token rather than the reverse), and `.edu` counts as a US country signal because `cc_of` already treats it that way.
  - **A hand-review REJECT keyed on one pairing cannot close the hole.** Four of the ten domains were ALREADY in `REJECT` - for a *different* seed. `northpark` was blocked for "North-West University" and then captured "University of North Texas"; `centralstate` was blocked for "Central European University" and captured "Central Florida". The list is a backstop, never the remedy.
  - **`clean_name` splitting on " - " is its own trap**: `instructure.charlotte.edu` publishes itself correctly as "University of North Carolina - Charlotte", and stripping the tail promoted a correct campus tenant into a claim on the whole system, one row above the real Chapel Hill. That is a `RENAME`, not a rejection - its own students need the entry.
- **The display name is `Local (English)`, and LOCAL_NAMES has a SECOND, invisible job.** A Dane types "Københavns", not "University of Copenhagen"; shipping only English made the picker miss on the first thing its user types. The same table is fed to the matcher as a **probe** (`local_probes`) - without it, an English seed and a locally-named account ("Göteborgs Universitet" vs "University of Gothenburg", which share *no* tokens and score 0.0) read as two different institutions and `contradicts` drops the school. **A display-only change would pass every rendering test and silently lose them.**
  - **Local names then made every Nordic row unreachable from a plain keyboard**, found by driving the running app: `kobenhavn`, `goteborg`, `hogskolan`, `lulea`, `haskolinn`, `nurnberg` each matched **zero** rows. `search_blob` now carries an accent-FOLDED copy beside the original, so a substring test finds the row either way and the bridge needs no change. Fold the HAYSTACK, not the query. `ø`/`æ`/`ß` are letters, not accented forms - NFKD leaves them alone, so they need an explicit map; `å` folds to `aa` (what people actually type) and still matches a `lulea` query by prefix. Known and accepted: an unaccented query finds the row but does not highlight it.
- **Score names SYMMETRICALLY, and never stop "college"/"state"/"university".** Both rules were learned by shipping their violation into a generated list. One-way recall of the seed's tokens scores 1.00 for any account merely CONTAINING the distinctive word - it paired **KTH with RMIT Melbourne, Korea University with Yamaha Music Korea, Cairo University with the American University in Cairo**. Stopping "college"/"state" collapses `University of Florida` and `Florida College` to the same token set - it paired **Boston University with Boston College, UC Berkeley with Berkeley College, and the University of Miami with Miami University (Ohio)**.
- **The domain must corroborate, by whole-LABEL equality or prefix - never substring** (`jesuit` contains `uit` and would vouch for UiT Norway on six high schools). Labels also strip a fused LMS affix, because institutions write `cbscanvas`: without that, **Copenhagen Business School's own domain fails to corroborate its own perfectly-matching name**. Aliases corroborate too - `Absalon` IS what `absalon.instructure.com` is called, and requiring the English seed there dropped Denmark's largest university from a Danish-market build.
- **A contradicting ccTLD is a decisive veto**, and it is the ONLY thing that rejects `Open University` (GB) matching `UTS Open` on `canvas.open.uts.edu.au` - the names genuinely share their one distinctive token.
- **Verification must prove Canvas, not reachability** (`/api/v1/users/self` returning Canvas's own unauthenticated payload): a parked domain, a marketing site and an SSO portal all answer an ordinary request. And **every failure is retried serially before being believed** - a 16-way sweep produced sporadic timeouts that silently deleted CBS, the University of Copenhagen and the University of Melbourne from a build, all three of which verify fine alone. `tests/test_institution_picker.py` pins those exact rows as ANCHORS, so a regeneration that loses a market fails instead of shipping.
- **`ui/institution_picker.py` is hand-built markup, not `st.selectbox`** - a Streamlit widget inside `st.form` cannot rerun, so a native picker could not fill the field until submit, which is the one moment the value is no longer needed.
- **The options are SERVER-rendered and the bridge only reads them.** A `components.html` iframe re-runs only when its srcdoc changes and this one's is constant, so anything the bridge painted would vanish the first time Streamlit re-rendered the markdown and never come back. The mirror image is why the picked label survives: React leaves an unchanged `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` subtree alone across reruns (verified - a full rerun kept the label, the filled URL and the status row).
- **Writing the value takes React's native setter plus `focus()` then `blur()`.** Assigning `.value` does not notify React; `blur()` is a no-op on an unfocused element. Verified end to end in the real app: picking CBS and submitting produced *"Access token needed"*, not *"Canvas URL needed"* - i.e. the value reached Python.
- **Corrected by measurement: `st.form` renders a DIV in 1.51, not a `<form>`** (zero `<form>` nodes on the login page), so a bare button here does not natively submit. `type="button"` and the Enter swallow stay anyway - Streamlit implements Enter-to-submit itself, so an un-swallowed Enter in the picker's search box still fires the login.
- **`match_url` is exact-host only**; a suffix rule would let `evil-harvard.edu` borrow `harvard.edu`'s identity. The live status row does offer a suggestion in the *opposite*, safe direction: typing `harvard.edu` (the main website, a natural mistake that fails at login with a generic error) names OUR verified `canvas.harvard.edu`. It prefers the SHORTEST host under the typed domain - taking the first match pointed Harvard at a pre-college certificate tenant.
- The picker's stylesheet lives in `ui/auth.py`'s login block. **Writing a literal angle-bracket tag name in one of those CSS comments kills the whole login stylesheet** - the architecture audit's Rule 7 caught exactly that while this was being built.
- **At ~1,800 entries the rendering model had to change, and the split is the point: the DATA is server-sent, the ROWS are not.** One button per institution is ~230 KB of markup for React to re-parse on every rerun plus 1,800 live nodes to filter per keystroke. The list now ships as ONE delimited payload on `.cd-inst-list[data-rows]` and the bridge renders only the top `RENDER_CAP` (60) matches. That is safe only because the rows are *derived* - a bridge runs once per mount, so anything it INVENTED would vanish on the next re-render and never come back, while anything it re-derives on open/keystroke cannot go stale. Delimiters are `»`/`«` (never present in a name, and unlike tabs/newlines they survive an attribute untouched); a test proves the shipped data contains neither.
- **`.instructure.com` ships as ONE byte, because rows have to be cheap.** Most tenants have no vanity domain, so those 16 characters repeat once in the domain field and again inside the search blob: measured on the 4,272-row list, 7,844 occurrences, 123 KB, **28% of the payload** - on the login page, which is the slowest screen in the app and the one a user reported hanging. `_SUF_TOKEN` (`*`) replaces it and the bridge's `expand()` restores it on BOTH fields at parse time, so the rendered domain, the `https://` link and the haystack are byte-identical to the uncompressed form; it runs once per mount because `rows()` memoises on the raw attribute. Net effect: **107 -> 79 bytes per row**, so 2.4x the institutions cost 1.7x the payload (187 KB -> 325 KB). `_clean_field` strips the token from names for the same reason it strips the delimiters - a literal `*` in a name would be expanded into `.instructure.com` and corrupt both the label and the search text. **The answer to payload pressure is cheaper rows, never fewer rows**: the picker is how nearly everyone finds their Canvas URL, and typing one by hand is the friction it exists to remove.
- Measured in the real app at 4,272 rows: **4.7-22.4 ms per keystroke**, i.e. unchanged from the ~1,800-row measurements, because `RENDER_CAP` bounds the DOM and the filter is a flat scan. (Re-measured at 4,750 rows WITH scoring and sorting: **3.1-15.8 ms**. See "The picker had no ranking" below - the filter is no longer a bare substring test.)
- **Two measured performance traps, both in the render path.** Compiling the highlight RegExp per ROW cost **55-78 ms per keystroke** (200 identical objects); it is now built once per render. And `scrollIntoView` forces a synchronous layout, so calling it on every render - for a list already scrolled to the top - cost another **35-60 ms**; it now fires only when the KEYBOARD moved the cursor. Final: worst keystroke **22.6 ms**, median 17.8, narrow queries ~5 ms.
- **The keyboard cursor must not be DOM focus.** Arrow keys move an `aria-activedescendant` on the search input, never focus itself, or typing stops working the moment you arrow into the list. The keyboard-active row is painted deliberately stronger than hover (plus a left rail): with a mouse hover and an arrow cursor both on screen, two identical highlights make it impossible to tell what Enter will take.
- **The three hand-review gates are different instruments, and picking the wrong one loses real users.** `REJECT` blocks a seed→domain PAIRING (the wrong-university case) but does NOT stop the fill re-adding that domain under its own correct name - which is right: `canvas.queens.edu` really is Queens University of Charlotte and its students should find it. `REJECT_DOMAINS` drops a host outright, for the AU/IE Catholic secondary schools that "College" cannot distinguish from real US liberal-arts colleges (enumerated, because any regex broad enough to catch `St Aloysius' College` also catches `Saint Anselm College`). `RENAME` is for a real tenant whose self-declared name OVER-claims - the only NYU account the finder publishes is Steinhardt's, named "New York University"; dropping it costs Steinhardt students their entry, so it ships as "New York University (Steinhardt)". **Prefer a rename to a rejection whenever the tenant is real and only the label is wrong.**
- ~~**Country is empty for ~83% of entries and that is not a defect**~~ - **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-09.** It was true while country only widened the search haystack; it stopped being acceptable the moment the picker began OPENING on the user's own country, because a country nobody has costs every user in it that whole feature. `infer_cc` now reads unambiguous markers in the institution's own name where the domain has no ccTLD, taking known countries from 366 to 1,556 (Denmark 2 -> 9). The ccTLD is still PROOF and always wins; a marker only ever speaks for a domain that has none, and the result is still never displayed as a claim about the institution - see below.
- **Three bugs shipped past per-feature verification, and all three lived at a SEAM** (found by the user, 2026-08-07). (a) `knownByHost` read the *rendered* dropdown rows, and closing the dropdown wipes them - so the act of PICKING a school made that school unknown and a freshly-picked Copenhagen Business School reported "Not one of the listed institutions". It reads the payload now. (b) The outside-click branch called `setOpen(false)` on **every click anywhere on the page**, and that focused the trigger - so clicking into the access-token field lit it up and then had focus yanked away a frame later, making the token impossible to type and login unusable. Closing an already-closed picker is now a no-op, and focus is restored only on a DELIBERATE close (Escape, or taking a row); an outside click leaves the caret where the user put it. (c) A `margin-top` written above a `margin` shorthand **in the same block** is silently overwritten - which made a spacing fix look like the CSS was not applying at all.
- **The status line groups with the field ABOVE it, and there is no standing "not listed" caveat.** The caveat now lives only in the picker's empty state, beside the search that came up empty - as a permanent line under the URL field it repeated that text verbatim and hung a "not listed" remark on a perfectly valid, recognised address. The status row itself is its own markdown block, so Streamlit's 1rem flow put 24px above it and 0 below, i.e. visually attached to the NEXT field's label; a single negative-top margin shorthand puts it at a measured 7px above / 12px below.
- **The trigger's label is DERIVED from the URL field, never remembered.** The field is the only truth - the picker exists solely to fill it - and storing the picked name separately let the two drift: clear the field after choosing a school and the picker still named a school the form no longer pointed at. `syncTrigger()` re-derives it from `paintStatus()`, which already fires on every keystroke, on pick and on mount; `pick()` must NOT write the label itself. Deriving it buys the reverse for free - paste a different known school's address by hand and the trigger follows. The default string lives in Python and is read off `data-default-label`, so the markup and the reset cannot disagree.

### The picker had no RANKING, and that was the whole problem (2026-08-09)
`matches()` filtered and returned rows in `DATA` order, which is `(country code, name)`. Everything below follows from that one omission, and all of it was measured in the running app before the fix.
- **What the user saw**: `harvard` answered with *"Harvard Medical School Pre-Med Online Certificate Program"*, `oxford` with *"Said Business School"*, `singapore` with *"National Institute of Early Childhood Development"*, and the picker OPENED on the United Arab Emirates and Australia for everybody on earth.
- **93% of the list was unreachable for a broad query.** 3,963 of 4,272 rows are `*.instructure.com`, which has no ccTLD, so `cc` was `""` and they sorted last under `'ZZ'`. `college` matched 929 rows and `school` 1,486, and a Filipino or Indian student never saw their school in either - the first 60 were always AE/AU/BD alphabetically.
- **The country blob poisoned the haystack.** `COUNTRY_NAMES` text was baked into every row, so `state` matched *"united states"*: **194 of its 322 hits matched nothing else**, and 232 of 535 for `us`. The table now ships ONCE (`country_payload()`) and a country term must match a WHOLE token - prefixes were tried and are wrong, because `state` prefixes `states`.
- **The weights are ORDINAL, not tuned**: whole name > name prefix > word in the name > fragment inside a word > domain > "your country is called this". A **fixed** bonus (seed, home country) must stay smaller than the gap between two adjacent tiers so it can only order near-equals; `test_ranking_weights_keep_their_order` pins that, and it is what stops a re-tune quietly restoring the unranked behaviour.
- **`S_DLAB` sits ABOVE a name prefix on purpose.** A whole domain label equal to the whole query is an institution stating its identity in four characters, and typing that acronym is how people who know their school look it up. Measured with it below the prefix score: `ust` answered with "UST Angelicum College" over the University of Santo Tomas on `ust.instructure.com`, and `tip` with "Tippecanoe School District" over the Technological Institute of the Philippines on `tip.instructure.com`.
- **The coverage bonus is the one signal that separates "contains the word" from "IS the word"** - the fraction of the NAME's own words the query accounted for. It is why "Harvard University" beats "Harvard Medical School Pre-Med Online Certificate Program" without any hand-written preference.
- **Prominence exists because no text score can express "many people mean this one".** `flags == "s"` marks a curated seed. It is NOT a level preference (the product owner ruled 2026-08-09 that K-12 must compete on equal footing) - a school district typing its own name wins on exactness and coverage long before the bonus matters. Without it, `melbourne` answers with Melbourne Grammar School purely because its name starts with the word.
- **A leading article costs a name its prefix bonus.** Canvas accounts are titled inconsistently ("The University of Melbourne" beside "University of Sydney"), and that alone decided the ranking. `article()` strips a leading definite/indefinite article for the prefix test only.
- **Mid-word matching is KEPT, at low weight.** It is what makes `roskilde` find `vucroskilde` and `akademi` find `Erhvervsakademi`; it is also why `unsw` matches "br**unsw**ick County Public Schools". Recall is worth having when ranking decides what is visible.
- **Education-word synonyms are scored LOW and are worth having**: they say what KIND of place this is, never which one. Without them "university jorge" cannot find "Universidade Jorge Amado" and a Dane's "universitet" cannot find an English-named row.

### The opening state, and reading the user's region offline
- Before a key is pressed the picker shows **the institutions in the user's own country**, seeds first, under *"Institutions in Denmark - or type to search all 4,750"*. The region comes from `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone` first and `navigator.language` second - **the time zone is asked first on purpose**: it reports where the machine IS, whereas the UI language is a preference, and an English-language Windows is the norm across most of this app's markets. No request is made and nothing is stored.
- Fewer than 5 rows for the region falls back to the curated seeds, which is a better "here is what this is" than an alphabetical slice.
- **The inferred country is never DISPLAYED as a claim about an institution** - it only widens the haystack and orders this list - which is what makes a lower evidence bar correct here than anywhere else in the builder. A wrong guess costs one unexpected row in a suggestion list; a wrong name-to-domain pairing costs a student their university.

### The pairing gates were reasoning about names with the disambiguator already deleted
Nine wrong name-to-domain pairings shipped, each verified against the live finder on 2026-08-09. **`norm_name` splits on `" - "` and strips parentheticals BEFORE every veto runs**, so "University of Tennessee - Martin" arrived as "University of Tennessee" and scored a perfect 1.00 against a seed it is not.
| Shipped as | Domain | What the account really says |
|---|---|---|
| University of South Carolina | `courses.online.usc.edu` | "USC Online" - Univ. of *Southern California* |
| Eotvos Lorand University | `elu.instructure.com` | "ELU - European Leadership University" |
| Universidad de los Andes (CO) | `uandes.instructure.com` | "Universidad de los Andes - **Chile**" |
| University of New South Wales | `canvas.unswcollege.edu.au` | "UNSW **College**" (a pathway provider) |
| University of Arizona | `healtharizona.instructure.com` | "... - College of Public Health" |
| University of Tennessee | `utm.instructure.com` | "... - **Martin**" |
| Western Sydney University | `canvas.westernsydneyonline.edu.au` | "Western Sydney **Online**" |
| University of York | `onlinestudy.york.ac.uk` | the online-study tenant |
| Georgia State University | `robinsongsu.instructure.com` | the Robinson College of Business tenant |
Knock-on: **University of Southern California was missing entirely**, because South Carolina had taken its domain and won the alphabetical dedupe.
- **`tail_of` / `tail_segments` recover what `norm_name` discards**, and there are now TWO questions asked of a tail, deliberately different. `tail_tokens` (broad) answers *"may I delete this from the LABEL?"* - it must stay broad or "University of Tennessee - Martin" becomes a claim on the whole university on the fill path. `qualifier_tokens` (narrow - only a tail carrying an entity-KIND word) answers *"is this a different institution?"*, and the narrowing was forced by measurement: a veto on every unvouched tail rejected "University of Michigan - **Ann Arbor**" while accepting "- **Flint**", because the branch campus names itself in its host and the flagship does not. **It reliably picked the wrong campus of any multi-campus university.** Coupling the two is what re-breaks either one.
- **An entity-KIND word the seed lacks is distinctive**, even though `_GENERIC` drops it from every other comparison. "UNSW College" is not the University of New South Wales, and *College* is the only word that says so.
- **An AMBIGUOUS initialism is not weak evidence, it is none.** `_AMBIGUOUS_ACRONYMS` is COMPUTED from the seed list (never hand-listed, so it cannot go stale) and the initialism branch declines anything in it. Note the test must use a host with no sub-tenant word in it - the shipped USC case is vetoed earlier by `domain_is_subtenant`, so asserting on it alone passes with the guard deleted.
- **A sub-tenant word in the DOMAIN is a veto only when the seed's own name does not contain it** - `online.smc.edu` really is Santa Monica College's Canvas. Declining is self-correcting: the domain falls through to the fill path and ships under the account's own name, so the institution is labelled rather than lost. `_TENANT_NOISE` cannot be reused here - it is written for RANKING, so its alternatives are unanchored and `pd`, `dev` and `prof` match inside ordinary words.
- **A hand-written `RENAME` is consulted BEFORE the name gates, not after.** Re-testing a human's answer with the heuristics they were overriding dropped four real tenants: `clean_name` strips a trailing "Online", which is the only education word "Western Sydney Online" and "USC Online" have.

### The crawl was silently discarding ~40% of every letter
- `crawl()` ran `range(1, 61)`. **The finder has no result cap**: measured by binary-searching the last non-empty page, `a` ends at 94 and `e` at 106. On letter `a` alone, pages 61-94 hold **2,433 domains the crawl never saw, 1,400 of which pass `is_institution`**.
- **a-z + 0-9 is PROVABLY complete**, and that is worth knowing before anyone adds market-specific search terms: the finder substring-matches the DOMAIN as well as the name (`oskilde` finds `vucroskilde.instructure.com`, whose account is named "VUC Roskilde"), so every `*.instructure.com` domain contains "instructure" and the single term `e` returns all 9,542 of them. Full crawl: **7,309 domains, 10,505 account names, ~15 minutes.**
- **A domain publishes SEVERAL names and the crawl kept whichever it reached first** - an artefact of alphabetical search order. 1,770 domains have more than one. `best_account_name` prefers a name carrying an education word over a bare acronym (`tip.instructure.com` shipped as "TIP" rather than "Technological Institute of the Philippines"), then one without an audience qualifier, then the longer.
- **`_BAD_ACCOUNT` is applied per NAME, and a domain is seedable if ANY of its names is clean.** `canvas.lms.unimelb.edu.au` publishes both "The University of Melbourne (non-SSO)" and "The University of Melbourne"; the first matched, so the seed found no candidate at all and Australia's best-known university shipped without its curated name - and, once ranking existed, below Melbourne Grammar School.
- **Verification verdicts are cached between runs, and only `ok` is ever cached.** A failure is the verdict that can be wrong for reasons outside the host, and a cached false negative would delete an institution permanently and identically on every later run. With the cache a rebuild is minutes instead of hours; the final retry pass went 1 worker -> 2, because a serial sweep over ~500 dead hosts at a 30 s timeout IS the runtime of the script.

### Coverage: the finder is NOT the ceiling - CORRECTED the same day
The paragraph that stood here claimed the shipped list was at the source's ceiling, on the strength of 1,529 conventional hostnames (`canvas.<domain>`, `lms.<domain>`, ...) answering zero times for India, South Africa and Nigeria. **That probe had a hole and the conclusion was wrong.** It never tested `<slug>.instructure.com`, which is how most tenants actually run. Re-probed properly:
